\section*{How to read this guide}\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{How to read this guide}

The file formalises Section 1 of the lecture notes (M.~Kroeker, \emph{Structural and Algorithmic
Graph Theory II}). Kuratowski's theorem says that for a graph $G$ the following are equivalent:
(1) $G$ is planar; (2) $G$ has no $K_5$- or $K_{3,3}$-subdivision; (3) $G$ has no $K_5$- or
$K_{3,3}$-minor. Section 1 of the notes proves (2)~$\Leftrightarrow$~(3), and so does the file:

\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{@{}lL@{}}
\toprule
Part 2 & subgraphs, vertex deletion $G - v$, edge deletion $G \setminus e$\\
Part 3 & edge contraction $G / uv$, Remark 1.2\\
Part 4 & models and minors (Definition 1.3), Remark 1.1 (1), “a minor of a minor is a minor”\\
Part 5 & minors as sequences of deletions and contractions (Proposition 1.4, both directions)\\
Part 6 & subdivisions, Remark 1.1 (2): a subdivision gives a minor\\
Part 7 & Propositions 1.5 and 1.6 (stated, proofs left as \lean{sorry})\\
Part 8 & Kuratowski (2)~$\Leftrightarrow$~(3), the minor version of Kuratowski, Remark 1.7\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}
\end{center}

Every declaration (\lean{def}, \lean{theorem}, \lean{example}, \lean{structure}, \lean{inductive})
gets its own subsection: first the code, then \textbf{Statement} (what it says in ordinary
mathematics), \textbf{Proof, step by step}, and \textbf{Tactics and tools} (what each tactic or
term-mode trick does at that point). Long proofs are split into several boxes. The appendix
at the end is an alphabetical glossary of every tactic and tool used in Parts 2--8.

\subsection*{Six things that are used everywhere}

\hd{1. Graphs.} A graph is a term \lean{G : SimpleGraph V}, where the type \lean{V} is the vertex set.
\lean{G.Adj x y} is the \emph{proposition} “$xy$ is an edge”. Mathlib's simple graphs have no loops
and no parallel edges; \lean{G.adj\_\allowbreak{}symm : G.Adj x y → G.Adj y x} and
\lean{G.ne\_\allowbreak{}of\_\allowbreak{}adj : G.Adj x y → x ≠ y}.

\hd{2. Definitional unfolding.} Lean checks proofs \emph{up to unfolding definitions}. Many
statements below are proved by \lean{Iff.rfl} (“both sides are the same after unfolding”), and
\lean{exact h} often works although the type of \lean{h} looks different from the goal.

\hd{3. Subtypes.} Deleting a vertex changes the vertex type. \lean{\{w : V // w ≠ v\}} is the type of
pairs \lean{⟨w, hw⟩} with \lean{w : V} and \lean{hw : w ≠ v}. For \lean{a} in it, \lean{a.1} (also
written \lean{↑a} or \lean{a.val}) is the vertex and \lean{a.2} the proof. Two elements are equal as
soon as their \lean{.1}s are equal: \lean{Subtype.ext : a.1 = b.1 → a = b}. A set
\lean{s : Set V} is turned into the type \lean{↥s = \{x // x ∈ s\}} whenever a type is expected.

\hd{4. Anonymous constructors.} \lean{⟨p, q⟩} builds whatever the goal needs: an \lean{∧}, an
\lean{∃} (\lean{⟨witness, proof⟩}), an \lean{↔} (\lean{⟨mp, mpr⟩}), a subtype element, or a structure.
Nested ones may be flattened: \lean{⟨a, ha, b, hb, h⟩} proves \lean{∃ a ∈ s, ∃ b ∈ t, P a b}.
Dually, \lean{rintro}/\lean{rcases}/\lean{obtain} take such terms apart with the same patterns.

\hd{5. Dot notation.} \lean{G.contract u v} means \lean{SimpleGraph.contract G u v}. A theorem named
\lean{IsMinor.trans} can be used as \lean{h.trans h'} whenever \lean{h : X.IsMinor H}. Likewise
\lean{h.1}/\lean{h.2} are the two directions of an \lean{↔} (or the two halves of an \lean{∧}),
and \lean{h.symm}, \lean{h.trans} are symmetry and transitivity of \lean{=}, \lean{≠},
\lean{Reachable}, \ldots

\hd{6. Explicit and implicit variables.} Line 222 says \lean{variable (G : SimpleGraph V)}: from then
on \lean{G} is an \emph{explicit} argument of every declaration that mentions it. Inside
\lean{section minors} etc.\ the file writes \lean{variable \{G\}}, which makes it \emph{implicit}
(Lean finds it from the other arguments), and \lean{variable (G) in} makes it explicit again for
the single next declaration.

% =====================================================================
\setcounter{section}{1}
\section{Part 2: subgraphs, vertex deletion and edge deletion}

The notes say: $H$ is a subgraph of $G$ iff $H$ can be obtained from $G$ by deleting edges and
vertices. In Lean there are two notions. If $H$ and $G$ have the same vertex type, \lean{H ≤ G}
says that every edge of $H$ is an edge of $G$. The induced subgraph $G[s]$ on a set
\lean{s : Set V} is \lean{G.induce s}, a graph whose vertex type is \lean{↥s}. Deleting the vertex
$v$ means passing to $G[V \setminus \{v\}]$.

\decl{\lean{le\_\allowbreak{}iff\_\allowbreak{}forall\_\allowbreak{}adj}}{theorem}{le\_iff\_forall\_adj}
\begin{leancode}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},numbers=left,firstnumber=222]
\PY{k+kn}{variable}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{SimpleGraph}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{V}\PY{o}{)}

\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ `H ≤ G` unfolds to \PYZdq{}every edge of `H` is an edge of `G`\PYZdq{}. }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}
\PY{k+kn}{theorem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{le\PYZus{}iff\PYZus{}forall\PYZus{}adj}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{H}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{SimpleGraph}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{V}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{H}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{≤}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{↔}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∀}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{y}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{H}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Adj}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{y}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{→}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Adj}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{y}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n}{Iff}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{rfl}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\says For two graphs on the same vertex type, $H \le G$ iff every edge $xy$ of $H$ is an edge of $G$.

\how The whole proof is the term \lean{Iff.rfl}. In Mathlib, \lean{H ≤ G} is \emph{defined} to be
\lean{∀ ⦃x y⦄, H.Adj x y → G.Adj x y}, so both sides of the \lean{↔} are the same proposition after
unfolding, and \lean{Iff.rfl : P ↔ P} is accepted. (The two sides use different kinds of
binders, \lean{⦃x y⦄} versus \lean{∀ x y}, but the kind of binder does not matter for definitional
equality.)

\tacs \lean{Iff.rfl} proves \lean{P ↔ Q} when \lean{P} and \lean{Q} are definitionally equal.
\lean{variable} declares \lean{G} for the rest of the file (see point 6 above). The point of stating
this theorem is to have a name that \lean{rw} can use, see the next example.

\decl{Example: using \lean{H ≤ G} in tactic mode}{example}{Example: using H ≤ G in tactic mode}
\begin{leancode}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},numbers=left,firstnumber=229]
\PY{k+kn}{example}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{H}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{SimpleGraph}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{V}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{H}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{≤}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{x}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{y}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{V}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{hxy}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{H}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Adj}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{y}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Adj}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{y}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{by}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nf}{rw}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{[}\PY{n}{le\PYZus{}iff\PYZus{}forall\PYZus{}adj}\PY{o}{]}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{at}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nf}{specialize}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{y}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nf}{apply}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hxy}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\says If $H \le G$ and $xy$ is an edge of $H$, then $xy$ is an edge of $G$.

\how
\begin{enumerate}
\item \lean{rw [le\_\allowbreak{}iff\_\allowbreak{}forall\_\allowbreak{}adj] at h} rewrites the hypothesis \lean{h : H ≤ G} with the
  \lean{↔} just proved; now \lean{h : ∀ x y, H.Adj x y → G.Adj x y}. (\lean{rw} rewrites with an
  \lean{↔} exactly as with an \lean{=}.)
\item \lean{specialize h x y} plugs in the two vertices: \lean{h : H.Adj x y → G.Adj x y}.
\item \lean{apply h}: the goal \lean{G.Adj x y} is the conclusion of \lean{h}, so it is enough to prove
  the premise; the goal becomes \lean{H.Adj x y}.
\item \lean{exact hxy} closes this goal.
\end{enumerate}

\tacs \lean{rw [lemma] at h} (rewrite a hypothesis), \lean{specialize h a b} (instantiate a
\lean{∀}-hypothesis), \lean{apply h} (reduce the goal to the premises of \lean{h}), \lean{exact}.
Why is the \lean{rw} needed? Mathlib's binders \lean{⦃x y⦄} are \emph{strict implicit}: they are
filled in automatically and cannot be given by position, so \lean{specialize h x y} on the
original \lean{h} would fail. After the rewrite, \lean{x} and \lean{y} are ordinary explicit
arguments.

\decl{Example: the same in term mode}{example}{Example: the same in term mode}
\begin{leancode}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},numbers=left,firstnumber=236]
\PY{k+kn}{example}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{H}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{SimpleGraph}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{V}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{H}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{≤}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{x}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{y}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{V}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{hxy}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{H}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Adj}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{y}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Adj}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{y}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hxy}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\how A proof of \lean{H ≤ G} \emph{is} a function: given a proof of \lean{H.Adj x y} it returns a
proof of \lean{G.Adj x y}. So \lean{h hxy} is the answer. The vertices \lean{x}, \lean{y} are the
strict-implicit arguments \lean{⦃x y⦄}; Lean reads them off from the type of \lean{hxy}.

\tacs Term mode: the proof is written directly as a term (no \lean{by}). Function application
\lean{h hxy} is “modus ponens”.

\decl{\lean{\#check} lines and induced subgraphs}{commands}{\#check lines and induced subgraphs}
\begin{leancode}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},numbers=left,firstnumber=239]
\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZsh{}}\PY{n}{check}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{@}\PY{n}{SimpleGraph}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{induce}\PY{+w}{      }\PY{c+c1}{\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{} `G.induce s : SimpleGraph ↥s`}
\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZsh{}}\PY{n}{check}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{@}\PY{n}{SimpleGraph}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{induce\PYZus{}adj}\PY{+w}{  }\PY{c+c1}{\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{} `(G.induce s).Adj a b ↔ G.Adj ↑a ↑b`}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\lean{\#check @name} prints the type of \lean{name}; the \lean{@} makes Lean show all arguments,
including the implicit ones. Lean prints:
\begin{leansays}
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
@induce : {V : Type u_4} → (s : Set V) → SimpleGraph V → SimpleGraph ↑s
@induce_adj : ∀ {V : Type u_4} {G : SimpleGraph V} {s : Set V} {u v : ↑s},
  (induce s G).Adj u v ↔ G.Adj ↑u ↑v
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leansays}
So \lean{G.induce s} is a graph on the type \lean{↑s} (the elements of \lean{s}), and two of its
vertices are adjacent iff the underlying vertices of \lean{G} are.

\begin{leancode}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},numbers=left,firstnumber=244]
\PY{k+kn}{example}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{s}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Set}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{V}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{a}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{b}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{s}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{G}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{induce}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{s}\PY{o}{)}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Adj}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{a}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{b}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{↔}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Adj}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{a}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{b}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n}{Iff}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{rfl}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\says In $G[s]$ two vertices are adjacent iff they are adjacent in $G$: an induced subgraph keeps
\emph{all} edges between its vertices. Here \lean{a b : s} are elements of the set \lean{s}, and in
\lean{G.Adj a b} Lean silently inserts the coercions \lean{↑a}, \lean{↑b} (the underlying vertices).

\how \lean{Iff.rfl}: \lean{G.induce s} is defined as the pull-back of \lean{G} along the inclusion
\lean{↑s → V}, and adjacency in a pull-back is, by definition, adjacency of the images.

\decl{\lean{deleteVert}}{definition}{deleteVert}
\begin{leancode}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},numbers=left,firstnumber=247]
\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ `G }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ v`: the graph obtained from `G` by deleting the vertex `v` (and all edges at `v`).}
\PY{l+s+sd}{Its vertices are the terms `⟨w, hw⟩` with `w : V` and `hw : w ≠ v`. }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}
\PY{k+kn}{def}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{deleteVert}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{v}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{V}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{SimpleGraph}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{w}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{V}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{/}\PY{n+nb+bp}{/}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{w}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{≠}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{v}\PY{o}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{induce}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{w}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{|}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{w}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{≠}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{v}\PY{o}{\PYZcb{}}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\says \lean{G.deleteVert v} is the graph $G - v$: the induced subgraph on $\{w \mid w \ne v\}$.
Its vertex type is the subtype \lean{\{w : V // w ≠ v\}}.

\how This is a \lean{def}, not a theorem: it \emph{constructs} a graph. The right-hand side
\lean{G.induce \{w | w ≠ v\}} has type \lean{SimpleGraph ↥\{w | w ≠ v\}}, and Lean accepts the
declared type \lean{SimpleGraph \{w : V // w ≠ v\}} because \lean{↥\{w | w ≠ v\}} is
\lean{\{w // w ∈ \{w | w ≠ v\}\}}, and \lean{w ∈ \{w | w ≠ v\}} unfolds to \lean{w ≠ v}.

\tacs \lean{def} introduces a definition. Because the file is inside \lean{namespace SimpleGraph}
and \lean{G} is the first explicit argument, the full name is \lean{SimpleGraph.deleteVert} and we
may write \lean{G.deleteVert v}. Set-builder notation \lean{\{w | P w\}} is a \lean{Set V}; the
subtype \lean{\{w : V // P w\}} is a \emph{type}.

\decl{\lean{deleteVert\_\allowbreak{}adj}}{theorem}{deleteVert\_adj}
\begin{leancode}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},numbers=left,firstnumber=251]
\PY{k+kn}{theorem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{deleteVert\PYZus{}adj}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{v}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{V}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{a}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{b}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{w}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{V}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{/}\PY{n+nb+bp}{/}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{w}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{≠}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{v}\PY{o}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{G}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{deleteVert}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{v}\PY{o}{)}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Adj}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{a}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{b}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{↔}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Adj}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{a}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{b}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n}{Iff}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{rfl}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\says Two vertices of $G - v$ are adjacent iff they are adjacent in $G$.

\how \lean{Iff.rfl}, because \lean{deleteVert} is \lean{induce} and the previous example applies.
The lemma exists so that later proofs can \lean{rw [deleteVert\_\allowbreak{}adj] at h} instead of relying on
unfolding.

\decl{Example: vertices of \lean{G - v}}{example}{Example: vertices of G - v}
\begin{leancode}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},numbers=left,firstnumber=255]
\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ Every vertex `w ≠ v` of `G` is still a vertex of `G }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ v`. (Lecture 3: `⟨w, h⟩` is a term of}
\PY{l+s+sd}{the subtype `\PYZob{}x : V }\PY{l+s+sd}{/}\PY{l+s+sd}{/}\PY{l+s+sd}{ x ≠ v\PYZcb{}`.) }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}
\PY{k+kn}{example}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{v}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{w}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{V}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{w}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{≠}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{v}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{x}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{V}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{/}\PY{n+nb+bp}{/}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{≠}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{v}\PY{o}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{w}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\says A vertex $w \ne v$ of $G$ is a vertex of $G - v$.

\how The anonymous constructor \lean{⟨w, h⟩} packs the vertex \lean{w} and the proof
\lean{h : w ≠ v} into one element of the subtype. Note that the \emph{statement} here is a type,
\lean{\{x : V // x ≠ v\}}, not a proposition: the example constructs data.

\decl{Edge deletion in Mathlib}{commands}{Edge deletion in Mathlib}
\begin{leancode}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},numbers=left,firstnumber=266]
\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZsh{}}\PY{n}{check}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{@}\PY{n}{SimpleGraph}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{deleteEdges}
\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZsh{}}\PY{n}{check}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{@}\PY{n}{SimpleGraph}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{deleteEdges\PYZus{}adj}\PY{+w}{  }\PY{c+c1}{\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{} `(G.deleteEdges s).Adj a b ↔ G.Adj a b ∧ s(a, b) ∉ s`}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
Mathlib already has edge deletion: \lean{G.deleteEdges s} deletes a set \lean{s : Set (Sym2 V)}
of edges. \lean{Sym2 V} is the type of \emph{unordered pairs}: \lean{s(x, y) : Sym2 V}, and
\lean{s(x, y) = s(y, x)} (\lean{Sym2.eq\_\allowbreak{}swap}). The two \lean{\#check}s print
\begin{leansays}
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
@deleteEdges : {V : Type u_4} → SimpleGraph V → Set (Sym2 V) → SimpleGraph V
@deleteEdges_adj : ∀ {V : Type u_4} {v w : V} {G : SimpleGraph V} {s : Set (Sym2 V)},
  (G.deleteEdges s).Adj v w ↔ G.Adj v w ∧ s(v, w) ∉ s
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leansays}
Deleting a single edge $e$, $G \setminus e$, is \lean{G.deleteEdges \{e\}}. Note that, unlike vertex
deletion, edge deletion does not change the vertex type.

\decl{\lean{deleteEdge\_\allowbreak{}adj}}{theorem}{deleteEdge\_adj}
\begin{leancode}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},numbers=left,firstnumber=269]
\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ `ab` is an edge of `G \PYZbs{} e` iff it is an edge of `G` different from `e`. }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}
\PY{k+kn}{theorem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{deleteEdge\PYZus{}adj}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{e}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Sym2}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{V}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{a}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{b}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{V}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{G}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{deleteEdges}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{e}\PY{o}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{o}{)}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Adj}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{a}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{b}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{↔}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Adj}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{a}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{b}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∧}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{s}\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{a}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{b}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{≠}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{e}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{by}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nf}{rw}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{[}\PY{n}{deleteEdges\PYZus{}adj}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Set}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{mem\PYZus{}singleton\PYZus{}iff}\PY{o}{]}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\says $ab$ is an edge of $G \setminus e$ iff $ab$ is an edge of $G$ and $ab \ne e$.

\how One \lean{rw} with two lemmas.
\begin{enumerate}
\item \lean{deleteEdges\_\allowbreak{}adj} turns the left side into \lean{G.Adj a b ∧ s(a, b) ∉ \{e\}}.
\item \lean{∉} is notation for \lean{¬ (\_\allowbreak{} ∈ \_\allowbreak{})}, and \lean{Set.mem\_\allowbreak{}singleton\_\allowbreak{}iff : x ∈ \{y\} ↔ x = y}
  turns it into \lean{¬ s(a, b) = e}.
\item Now the two sides are \lean{G.Adj a b ∧ ¬ s(a, b) = e} and \lean{G.Adj a b ∧ s(a, b) ≠ e}.
  \lean{x ≠ y} is by definition \lean{¬ x = y}, and \lean{rw} always tries \lean{rfl} after
  rewriting, which closes the goal. That is why there is no further line.
\end{enumerate}

\tacs \lean{rw [h₁, h₂]} rewrites with \lean{h₁}, then with \lean{h₂}, then tries \lean{rfl}.

\decl{Example: \lean{G \textbackslash{} s ≤ G}}{example}{Example: G \ s ≤ G}
\begin{leancode}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},numbers=left,firstnumber=275]
\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ `G \PYZbs{} e` is a subgraph of `G`. (This is `deleteEdges\PYZus{}le` in Mathlib.) }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}
\PY{k+kn}{example}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{s}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Set}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{Sym2}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{V}\PY{o}{)}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{deleteEdges}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{s}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{≤}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{by}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nf}{intro}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{y}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nf}{rw}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{[}\PY{n}{deleteEdges\PYZus{}adj}\PY{o}{]}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{at}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nf}{rcases}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{with}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{h}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\says Deleting edges gives a subgraph. (Mathlib calls this \lean{deleteEdges\_\allowbreak{}le}.)

\how
\begin{enumerate}
\item \lean{intro x y h}: the goal \lean{G.deleteEdges s ≤ G} unfolds to a \lean{∀}-statement, so
  \lean{intro} can introduce the vertices and \lean{h : (G.deleteEdges s).Adj x y}.
\item \lean{rw [deleteEdges\_\allowbreak{}adj] at h} gives \lean{h : G.Adj x y ∧ s(x, y) ∉ s}.
\item \lean{rcases h with ⟨h, -⟩} splits the \lean{∧}; the pattern \lean{-} throws the second half away.
\item \lean{exact h}.
\end{enumerate}

\tacs \lean{intro} also works when the goal is only a \lean{∀} \emph{after unfolding}. In
\lean{rcases} patterns, \lean{⟨p, q⟩} splits an \lean{∧} (or \lean{∃}), and \lean{-} clears a component.

% =====================================================================
\section{Part 3: edge contraction}

Let $e = uv$. The notes define $G / e$ by deleting $e$, identifying $u$ and $v$ to a new vertex
$w$, and letting $w$ inherit all edges of $u$ and $v$. In Lean, the old vertex $u$ plays the
role of $w$ and $v$ is deleted, so $G / uv$ lives on the same vertex type
\lean{\{w : V // w ≠ v\}} as $G - v$. Two vertices $a \ne b$ are adjacent in $G / uv$ iff
\begin{itemize}
\item $ab$ is an edge of $G$, or
\item $a = u$ and $vb$ is an edge of $G$ ($u$ inherits the neighbours of $v$), or
\item $b = u$ and $av$ is an edge of $G$.
\end{itemize}
The definition makes sense for any $u, v$ (for $uv \notin E(G)$ it identifies $u$ and $v$). Since
simple graphs have no parallel edges, an edge $ub$ and an edge $vb$ become \emph{one} edge $ub$,
and no loop is ever created.

\decl{\lean{contract}}{definition}{contract}
\begin{leancode}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},numbers=left,firstnumber=300]
\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ `G }\PY{l+s+sd}{/}\PY{l+s+sd}{ uv`: contract the edge `uv` to the vertex `u`. }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}
\PY{k+kn}{def}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{contract}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{u}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{v}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{V}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{SimpleGraph}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{w}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{V}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{/}\PY{n+nb+bp}{/}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{w}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{≠}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{v}\PY{o}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k+kn}{where}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n}{Adj}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{a}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{b}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n}{a}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{1}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{≠}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{b}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{1}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∧}
\PY{+w}{      }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{G}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Adj}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{a}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{1}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{b}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{1}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∨}
\PY{+w}{        }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{a}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{1}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{u}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∧}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Adj}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{v}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{b}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{1}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∨}
\PY{+w}{        }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{b}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{1}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{u}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∧}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Adj}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{a}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{1}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{v}\PY{o}{)}\PY{o}{)}

\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n}{symm}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{k}{by}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nf}{rintro}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{a}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{b}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{hab}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nf}{refine}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{hab}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{symm}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{?}\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZus{}}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nf}{rcases}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{with}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{|}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{ha}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hb}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{|}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{hb}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ha}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nb+bp}{·}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Or}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{inl}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{G}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{adj\PYZus{}symm}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{o}{)}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nb+bp}{·}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Or}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{inr}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{Or}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{inr}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{ha}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{adj\PYZus{}symm}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hb}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}\PY{o}{)}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nb+bp}{·}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Or}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{inr}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{Or}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{inl}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{hb}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{adj\PYZus{}symm}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ha}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}\PY{o}{)}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}

\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n}{loopless}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{k}{by}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nf}{rintro}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{a}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{haa}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZus{}}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{haa}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{rfl}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\says \lean{G.contract u v} is the graph $G / uv$ described above.

\how A \lean{SimpleGraph} is a structure with three fields, and \lean{where} gives them one by one:
\begin{itemize}
\item \lean{Adj a b} (lines 302--306) is the adjacency relation. The first conjunct
  \lean{a.1 ≠ b.1} is needed: without it, if $vu$ is an edge then the second case (with
  $a = b = u$) would make $u$ adjacent to itself, and simple graphs have no loops.
\item \lean{symm} (lines 308--314) proves that \lean{Adj} is symmetric. In the current Mathlib this
  field has type \lean{Std.Symm Adj}, a one-field structure around
  \lean{∀ a b, Adj a b → Adj b a}; that is why the tactic proof is wrapped in \lean{⟨by ...⟩}.
  \begin{itemize}
  \item \lean{rintro a b ⟨hab, h⟩} introduces \lean{a}, \lean{b} and splits \lean{Adj a b} into
    \lean{hab : a.1 ≠ b.1} and the three-way disjunction \lean{h}.
  \item \lean{refine ⟨hab.symm, ?\_\allowbreak{}⟩}: the first half of \lean{Adj b a} is \lean{b.1 ≠ a.1},
    which is \lean{hab.symm}; the \lean{?\_\allowbreak{}} is left as the new goal.
  \item \lean{rcases h with h | ⟨ha, hb⟩ | ⟨hb, ha⟩} splits the disjunction
    \lean{A ∨ (B ∨ C)} into its three cases (and each \lean{∧} into two hypotheses).
  \item Case 1 (\lean{h : G.Adj a.1 b.1}): \lean{Or.inl (G.adj\_\allowbreak{}symm h)}.
    Case 2 (\lean{ha : a.1 = u}, \lean{hb : G.Adj v b.1}): for \lean{Adj b a} this is the
    \emph{third} case, \lean{Or.inr (Or.inr ⟨ha, G.adj\_\allowbreak{}symm hb⟩)}.
    Case 3 is symmetric and becomes the second case.
  \end{itemize}
\item \lean{loopless} (lines 316--318) proves \lean{∀ a, ¬ Adj a a} (field type
  \lean{Std.Irrefl Adj}). Since \lean{¬ P} means \lean{P → False}, \lean{rintro a ⟨haa, \_\allowbreak{}⟩}
  introduces \lean{a} and splits the hypothesis \lean{Adj a a}; \lean{haa : a.1 ≠ a.1}, and
  \lean{haa rfl : False}.
\end{itemize}

\tacs \lean{where} (structure fields); \lean{rintro} with patterns; \lean{refine} with a hole
\lean{?\_\allowbreak{}}; \lean{rcases} with \lean{|} for \lean{∨}; \lean{Or.inl}/\lean{Or.inr} choose the left/right
side of an \lean{∨} (for a three-fold \lean{A ∨ B ∨ C}, the second case is
\lean{Or.inr (Or.inl \_\allowbreak{})} and the third \lean{Or.inr (Or.inr \_\allowbreak{})}); \lean{rfl : x = x}; the bullets
\lean{·} focus on one goal at a time.

\begin{leancode}

\PY{k+kn}{theorem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{contract\PYZus{}adj}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{u}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{v}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{V}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{a}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{b}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{w}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{V}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{/}\PY{n+nb+bp}{/}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{w}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{≠}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{v}\PY{o}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{G}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{contract}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{u}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{v}\PY{o}{)}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Adj}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{a}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{b}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{↔}
\PY{+w}{      }\PY{n}{a}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{1}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{≠}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{b}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{1}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∧}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{G}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Adj}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{a}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{1}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{b}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{1}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∨}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{a}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{1}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{u}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∧}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Adj}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{v}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{b}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{1}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∨}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{b}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{1}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{u}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∧}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Adj}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{a}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{1}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{v}\PY{o}{)}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n}{Iff}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{rfl}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\says The definition of adjacency in $G / uv$, as a lemma that \lean{rw} and \lean{simp} can use.
\how \lean{Iff.rfl}: the right-hand side is literally the field \lean{Adj} of the definition.

\PY{+w}{    }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{G}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{contract}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{u}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{v}\PY{o}{)}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Adj}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{a}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{b}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{G}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{ne\PYZus{}of\PYZus{}adj}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Or}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{inl}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\says If $ab$ is an edge of $G$ and $a, b \ne v$, then $ab$ is an edge of $G / uv$.

\how Term mode. The goal \lean{(G.contract u v).Adj a b} unfolds to
\lean{a.1 ≠ b.1 ∧ (G.Adj a.1 b.1 ∨ ...)}, so the anonymous constructor \lean{⟨\_\allowbreak{}, \_\allowbreak{}⟩} builds the
\lean{∧}: the first half is \lean{G.ne\_\allowbreak{}of\_\allowbreak{}adj h} (an edge joins two different vertices), the second
half is the first case of the disjunction, \lean{Or.inl h}. (Here \lean{h : G.Adj a b} with
\lean{a b} in the subtype means \lean{G.Adj ↑a ↑b}.)

\tacs anonymous constructor for \lean{∧}; \lean{Or.inl}; \lean{G.ne\_\allowbreak{}of\_\allowbreak{}adj}.

\end{Verbatim}

\says $G - v$ is a subgraph of $G / uv$ (both have vertex type \lean{\{w // w ≠ v\}}).

\how \lean{intro a b h} (as \lean{≤} is a \lean{∀}); \lean{rw [deleteVert\_\allowbreak{}adj] at h} turns
\lean{h} into \lean{G.Adj ↑a ↑b}; the previous theorem finishes. In
\lean{G.contract\_\allowbreak{}adj\_\allowbreak{}of\_\allowbreak{}adj u v h}, \lean{G}, \lean{u}, \lean{v} are the explicit arguments and
\lean{a}, \lean{b} (in curly braces in the statement) are found from \lean{h}.

\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\says The new vertex $u$ inherits the neighbours of $v$: if $vb \in E(G)$ and $b \ne u$, then
$ub \in E(G / uv)$. Here \lean{⟨u, huv⟩} is $u$ regarded as a vertex of $G / uv$.

\how Again an anonymous constructor for the \lean{∧}:
\begin{itemize}
\item first half, \lean{u ≠ b.1}: a proof of \lean{≠} is a function from \lean{u = b.1} to
  \lean{False}; \lean{fun h' ↦ hb h'.symm} turns \lean{h' : u = b.1} into \lean{b.1 = u} and feeds it
  to \lean{hb : b.1 ≠ u};
\item second half: the second case of the disjunction, \lean{Or.inr (Or.inl ⟨rfl, h⟩)}, where
  \lean{rfl : u = u} and \lean{h : G.Adj v b}.
\end{itemize}

\tacs \lean{fun x ↦ ...} to prove a \lean{¬}/\lean{≠}; \lean{.symm}; nested \lean{Or.inr (Or.inl \_\allowbreak{})}.

\decl{\lean{deleteEdges\_\allowbreak{}contract} (Remark 1.2 (2))}{theorem}{deleteEdges\_contract (Remark 1.2 (2))}

\PY{k+kn}{theorem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{deleteEdges\PYZus{}contract}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{u}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{v}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{V}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{s}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Set}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{Sym2}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{V}\PY{o}{)}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{hs}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∀}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{e}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∈}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{s}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{u}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∉}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{e}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∧}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{v}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∉}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{e}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{G}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{deleteEdges}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{s}\PY{o}{)}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{contract}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{u}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{v}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{=}
\PY{+w}{      }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{G}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{contract}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{u}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{v}\PY{o}{)}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{deleteEdges}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{Sym2}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{map}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Subtype}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{val}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⁻¹}\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{s}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{by}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nf}{ext}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{a}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ha}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{b}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hb}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nf}{simp}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{only}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{[}\PY{n}{contract\PYZus{}adj}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{deleteEdges\PYZus{}adj}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Set}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{mem\PYZus{}preimage}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Sym2}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{map\PYZus{}mk}\PY{o}{]}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nf}{constructor}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\says Edge deletion commutes with contraction: if no edge in $s$ has $u$ or $v$ as an end
(\lean{hs}), then $(G \setminus s) / uv = (G / uv) \setminus s$.

On the right, \lean{s} is a set of edges of $G$ (unordered pairs of vertices of \lean{V}), but
$G / uv$ has vertex type \lean{\{w // w ≠ v\}}. So we use the preimage
\lean{Sym2.map Subtype.val ⁻¹' s}: the map \lean{Sym2.map Subtype.val} sends the pair
\lean{s(a, b)} of vertices of $G / uv$ to the pair \lean{s(a.1, b.1)} of vertices of $G$, and
\lean{f ⁻¹' s} is the preimage of \lean{s} under \lean{f}.

\how
\begin{enumerate}
\item \lean{ext ⟨a, ha⟩ ⟨b, hb⟩}: two graphs are equal iff they have the same adjacency relation, so
  \lean{ext} reduces the equation to “\lean{Adj x y ↔ Adj' x y} for all vertices \lean{x}, \lean{y}”,
  and the patterns immediately split each vertex into the underlying vertex and its proof of
  \lean{≠ v}.
\item \lean{simp only [...]} unfolds everything: \lean{contract\_\allowbreak{}adj} and \lean{deleteEdges\_\allowbreak{}adj}
  are the definitions of adjacency, \lean{Set.mem\_\allowbreak{}preimage : x ∈ f ⁻¹' s ↔ f x ∈ s}, and
  \lean{Sym2.map\_\allowbreak{}mk : Sym2.map f s(x, y) = s(f x, f y)}. Lean then shows:
\begin{leansays}
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
a : V
ha : a ≠ v
b : V
hb : b ≠ v
⊢ a ≠ b ∧ (G.Adj a b ∧ s(a, b) ∉ s ∨ a = u ∧ G.Adj v b ∧ s(v, b) ∉ s ∨
           b = u ∧ G.Adj a v ∧ s(a, v) ∉ s) ↔
    (a ≠ b ∧ (G.Adj a b ∨ a = u ∧ G.Adj v b ∨ b = u ∧ G.Adj a v)) ∧ s(a, b) ∉ s
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leansays}
  This is pure propositional logic, apart from the hypothesis \lean{hs}.
\item \lean{constructor} splits the \lean{↔} into the two implications.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{leancode}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},numbers=left,firstnumber=356]

\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nf}{refine}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{hab}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{?}\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZus{}}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{?}\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZus{}}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nb+bp}{·}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{rcases}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{with}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{h}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{|}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{hau}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{|}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{hbu}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}
\PY{+w}{      }\PY{n+nb+bp}{·}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Or}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{inl}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}
\PY{+w}{      }\PY{n+nb+bp}{·}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Or}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{inr}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{Or}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{inl}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{hau}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}\PY{o}{)}
\PY{+w}{      }\PY{n+nb+bp}{·}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Or}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{inr}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{Or}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{inr}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{hbu}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}\PY{o}{)}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nb+bp}{·}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{rcases}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{with}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{|}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{hau}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{|}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{hbu}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}
\PY{+w}{      }\PY{n+nb+bp}{·}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}
\PY{+w}{      }\PY{n+nb+bp}{·}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{intro}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hmem}
\PY{+w}{        }\PY{n+nf}{apply}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{hs}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZus{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hmem}\PY{o}{)}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{1}
\PY{+w}{        }\PY{n+nf}{rw}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{[}\PY{n+nb+bp}{←}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hau}\PY{o}{]}
\PY{+w}{        }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Sym2}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{mem\PYZus{}mk\PYZus{}left}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{a}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{b}
\PY{+w}{      }\PY{n+nb+bp}{·}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{intro}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hmem}
\PY{+w}{        }\PY{n+nf}{apply}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{hs}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZus{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hmem}\PY{o}{)}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{1}
\PY{+w}{        }\PY{n+nf}{rw}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{[}\PY{n+nb+bp}{←}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hbu}\PY{o}{]}
\PY{+w}{        }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Sym2}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{mem\PYZus{}mk\PYZus{}right}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{a}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{b}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\how \emph{Left to right.} \lean{rintro ⟨hab, h⟩} takes the hypothesis apart; \lean{refine ⟨⟨hab, ?\_\allowbreak{}⟩, ?\_\allowbreak{}⟩}
leaves two goals: the disjunction, and \lean{s(a, b) ∉ s}.
\begin{itemize}
\item For the disjunction, \lean{rcases h with ⟨h, -⟩ | ⟨hau, h, -⟩ | ⟨hbu, h, -⟩} goes through the
  three cases and throws away the “not deleted” parts (\lean{-}); then \lean{Or.inl}/\lean{Or.inr}
  put the remaining facts in the right place.
\item For \lean{s(a, b) ∉ s} the same \lean{rcases} keeps the other parts. In case 1 this is exactly
  the hypothesis \lean{h}. In case 2 we know \lean{hau : a = u}. \lean{intro hmem} assumes
  \lean{s(a, b) ∈ s}; then \lean{(hs \_\allowbreak{} hmem).1 : u ∉ s(a, b)}, and \lean{apply} to the goal
  \lean{False} leaves the goal \lean{u ∈ s(a, b)}. \lean{rw [← hau]} rewrites \lean{u} into \lean{a}
  (the arrow \lean{←} rewrites from right to left), and \lean{Sym2.mem\_\allowbreak{}mk\_\allowbreak{}left a b : a ∈ s(a, b)}
  closes it. Case 3 is the same with \lean{b = u} and \lean{Sym2.mem\_\allowbreak{}mk\_\allowbreak{}right}.
\end{itemize}

\PY{+w}{      }\PY{n+nf}{intro}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hmem}
\PY{+w}{      }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{hs}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZus{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hmem}\PY{o}{)}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{2}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{Sym2}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{mem\PYZus{}mk\PYZus{}left}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{v}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{b}\PY{o}{)}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nb+bp}{·}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{refine}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Or}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{inr}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{Or}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{inr}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{hbu}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{?}\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZus{}}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}\PY{o}{)}
\PY{+w}{      }\PY{n+nf}{intro}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hmem}
\PY{+w}{      }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{hs}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZus{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hmem}\PY{o}{)}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{2}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{Sym2}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{mem\PYZus{}mk\PYZus{}right}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{a}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{v}\PY{o}{)}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\how \emph{Right to left.} Now we know \lean{hnot : s(a, b) ∉ s}, and must show that the edge that
produced adjacency in $G / uv$ was not deleted. Case 1 is \lean{Or.inl ⟨h, hnot⟩}. In case 2 the
edge used is \lean{s(v, b)}; if it were in \lean{s}, then \lean{(hs \_\allowbreak{} hmem).2 : v ∉ s(v, b)}
contradicts \lean{Sym2.mem\_\allowbreak{}mk\_\allowbreak{}left v b : v ∈ s(v, b)}. Case 3 is the same with \lean{s(a, v)}.

\tacs \lean{ext} (extensionality for graphs), \lean{simp only [lemmas]} (rewrite with exactly these
lemmas, as often as possible), \lean{constructor} (split an \lean{↔}), \lean{rintro}/\lean{rcases}
with \lean{|}, \lean{-} patterns, \lean{refine} with two holes, \lean{rw [← h]}, \lean{apply} on the
goal \lean{False} (a proof of \lean{¬ P} is a function \lean{P → False}).

\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n}{invFun}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{a}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{a}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{1}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{1}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{fun}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{↦}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{a}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{2}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{Subtype}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n+nf}{ext}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{o}{)}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{fun}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{↦}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{a}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{1}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{2}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{congrArg}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Subtype}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{val}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{o}{)}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n}{left\PYZus{}inv}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{a}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{rfl}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n}{right\PYZus{}inv}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{a}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{rfl}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\says Contracting first $uv$ and then $xy$ gives a graph \emph{isomorphic} to the one obtained by
contracting first $xy$ and then $uv$. The hypotheses \lean{x ≠ v}, \lean{y ≠ v}, \lean{u ≠ y},
\lean{v ≠ y} make sure that both orders make sense (e.g.\ after deleting $v$, the vertex $x$ must
still exist). The two edges may share the end $u = x$.

A graph isomorphism \lean{X ≃g Y} is a bijection between the vertex types (\lean{toFun},
\lean{invFun} and the proofs \lean{left\_\allowbreak{}inv}, \lean{right\_\allowbreak{}inv} that they are inverse) together
with \lean{map\_\allowbreak{}rel\_\allowbreak{}iff'}: it preserves adjacency in both directions.

\how The vertex type on the left is \lean{\{w : \{w : V // w ≠ v\} // w ≠ ⟨y, hyv⟩\}}: a vertex is a
vertex \lean{a.1.1 : V} with two proofs attached, \lean{a.1.2 : a.1.1 ≠ v} and
\lean{a.2 : a.1 ≠ ⟨y, hyv⟩}. On the right the two proofs are \lean{≠ y} (inner) and
\lean{≠ ⟨v, hvy⟩} (outer). So the bijection keeps the vertex \lean{a.1.1} and just converts the
two proofs:
\begin{itemize}
\item \lean{fun h ↦ a.2 (Subtype.ext h)}: from \lean{h : a.1.1 = y}, \lean{Subtype.ext h} gives
  \lean{a.1 = ⟨y, hyv⟩}, contradicting \lean{a.2}. So this proves \lean{a.1.1 ≠ y}.
\item \lean{fun h ↦ a.1.2 (congrArg Subtype.val h)}: from \lean{h : ⟨a.1.1, \_\allowbreak{}⟩ = ⟨v, hvy⟩},
  \lean{congrArg Subtype.val h : a.1.1 = v} (apply \lean{.val} to both sides), contradicting
  \lean{a.1.2}.
\end{itemize}
\lean{invFun} is the same code in the other direction. \lean{left\_\allowbreak{}inv} and \lean{right\_\allowbreak{}inv} hold by
\lean{rfl}: going there and back gives the same vertex with (possibly) different proofs, and any
two proofs of the same proposition are equal by definition (\emph{proof irrelevance}).

\begin{leancode}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},numbers=left,firstnumber=399]
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n}{map\PYZus{}rel\PYZus{}iff\PYZsq{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{by}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nf}{rintro}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{a}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hav}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hay}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{b}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hbv}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hby}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{k}{have}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hay\PYZsq{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{a}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{≠}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{y}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{fun}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{↦}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hay}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{Subtype}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n+nf}{ext}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{o}{)}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{k}{have}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hby\PYZsq{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{b}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{≠}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{y}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{fun}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{↦}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hby}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{Subtype}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n+nf}{ext}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{o}{)}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nf}{simp}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{only}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{[}\PY{n}{Equiv}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{coe\PYZus{}fn\PYZus{}mk}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{contract\PYZus{}adj}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Subtype}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{mk}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{injEq}\PY{o}{]}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nf}{grind}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\how \lean{map\_\allowbreak{}rel\_\allowbreak{}iff'} says: \lean{f a} and \lean{f b} are adjacent on the right iff \lean{a} and
\lean{b} are adjacent on the left.
\begin{enumerate}
\item \lean{rintro ⟨⟨a, hav⟩, hay⟩ ⟨⟨b, hbv⟩, hby⟩} introduces the two vertices and splits each
  one into the vertex of \lean{V} and its two proofs.
\item The two \lean{have}s convert \lean{hay : ⟨a, hav⟩ ≠ ⟨y, hyv⟩} (an inequality in the subtype)
  into \lean{hay' : a ≠ y} (an inequality in \lean{V}), which is what the unfolded goal talks about.
\item \lean{simp only [Equiv.coe\_\allowbreak{}fn\_\allowbreak{}mk, contract\_\allowbreak{}adj, Subtype.mk.injEq]} evaluates the function
  application (\lean{Equiv.coe\_\allowbreak{}fn\_\allowbreak{}mk}), unfolds adjacency in all four contractions
  (\lean{contract\_\allowbreak{}adj}), and replaces equations between subtype elements
  \lean{⟨p, \_\allowbreak{}⟩ = ⟨q, \_\allowbreak{}⟩} by \lean{p = q} (\lean{Subtype.mk.injEq}). What is left is:
\begin{leansays}
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\scriptsize]
hay' : a ≠ y
hby' : b ≠ y
⊢ ⟨a, ⋯⟩ ≠ ⟨b, ⋯⟩ ∧
      (a ≠ b ∧ (G.Adj a b ∨ a = x ∧ G.Adj y b ∨ b = x ∧ G.Adj a y) ∨
        a = u ∧ v ≠ b ∧ (G.Adj v b ∨ v = x ∧ G.Adj y b ∨ b = x ∧ G.Adj v y) ∨
          b = u ∧ a ≠ v ∧ (G.Adj a v ∨ a = x ∧ G.Adj y v ∨ v = x ∧ G.Adj a y)) ↔
    ⟨a, hav⟩ ≠ ⟨b, hbv⟩ ∧
      (a ≠ b ∧ (G.Adj a b ∨ a = u ∧ G.Adj v b ∨ b = u ∧ G.Adj a v) ∨
        a = x ∧ y ≠ b ∧ (G.Adj y b ∨ y = u ∧ G.Adj v b ∨ b = u ∧ G.Adj y v) ∨
          b = x ∧ a ≠ y ∧ (G.Adj a y ∨ a = u ∧ G.Adj v y ∨ y = u ∧ G.Adj a v))
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leansays}
  (The first lines of each side are “the two vertices are different”, still as an inequality of
  subtype elements; the rest is the three-case definition, used twice.)
\item \lean{grind} proves it. This is propositional logic plus reasoning with equalities
  (e.g.\ from \lean{a = u} and \lean{u = x} conclude \lean{a = x}), which \lean{grind} does
  automatically by case splitting and congruence closure.
\end{enumerate}

\tacs structure instance with \lean{where}; \lean{Subtype.ext}; \lean{congrArg f h : f x = f y} from
\lean{h : x = y}; \lean{rfl} with proof irrelevance; \lean{rintro} with nested patterns;
\lean{have}; \lean{simp only}; \lean{grind} (automation: case splits + equality reasoning).

% =====================================================================
\section{Part 4: models and minors}

For proofs, the most convenient description of minors is via \emph{models} (Definition 1.3 and
Proposition 1.4). An $X$-model in $G$ is a family of \emph{branch sets} $V_x \subseteq V(G)$, one
for each vertex $x$ of $X$, such that
(1) each $V_x$ is nonempty and the $V_x$ are pairwise disjoint;
(2) each $G[V_x]$ is connected;
(3) for every edge $xy$ of $X$ there is an edge of $G$ between $V_x$ and $V_y$.
The notes instead ask for a subgraph $H$ with trees $H[V_x]$ and exactly one edge between $V_x$
and $V_y$ for each edge $xy$; the two versions are equivalent (choose spanning trees and one edge
per edge of $X$; conversely, a tree is connected).

\decl{\lean{Model} (Definition 1.3)}{structure}{Model (Definition 1.3)}
\begin{leancode}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},numbers=left,firstnumber=429]
\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ Definition 1.3: an `X`}\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{model in `G`, given by its branch sets `branch x ⊆ V(G)`. }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}
\PY{k+kn}{structure}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Model}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{X}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{SimpleGraph}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{W}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{SimpleGraph}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{V}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k+kn}{where}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ The branch set `V\PYZus{}x` of the vertex `x` of `X`. }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n}{branch}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{W}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{→}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Set}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{V}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n}{nonempty}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∀}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{branch}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{o}{)}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Nonempty}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n}{disjoint}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∀}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{y}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{≠}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{y}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{→}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Disjoint}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{branch}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{branch}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{y}\PY{o}{)}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n}{connected}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∀}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{G}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{induce}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{branch}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{o}{)}\PY{o}{)}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Connected}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n}{adj}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∀}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{y}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{X}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Adj}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{y}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{→}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∃}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{a}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∈}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{branch}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∃}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{b}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∈}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{branch}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{y}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Adj}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{a}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{b}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\says An $X$-model in $G$ consists of the function \lean{branch : W → Set V} (the branch sets $V_x$)
and proofs of the three conditions above.

\how A \lean{structure} is a package of named fields: here one piece of data (\lean{branch}) and four
proofs. Given \lean{M : X.Model G}, we write \lean{M.branch x}, \lean{M.nonempty x},
\lean{M.disjoint x y hxy}, \lean{M.connected x}, \lean{M.adj x y hxy}. To build one we either use an
anonymous constructor \lean{⟨branch, nonempty, disjoint, connected, adj⟩} (Part 4) or
\lean{where} with one line per field (Part 5).

\tacs \lean{(branch x).Nonempty} means \lean{∃ a, a ∈ branch x}; \lean{Disjoint s t} for sets means
\lean{s ∩ t = ∅} (used via \lean{Set.disjoint\_\allowbreak{}left : Disjoint s t ↔ ∀ a ∈ s, a ∉ t});
\lean{(G.induce s).Connected} means that $G[s]$ is nonempty and any two of its vertices are
joined by a walk; \lean{∃ a ∈ s, P a} is short for \lean{∃ a, a ∈ s ∧ P a}.

\decl{\lean{IsMinor}}{definition}{IsMinor}
\begin{leancode}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},numbers=left,firstnumber=438]
\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ `G` has an `X`}\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{minor (`X` is a minor of `G`): there exists an `X`}\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{model in `G`. By}
\PY{l+s+sd}{Proposition 1.4 this is the same as saying that a graph isomorphic to `X` can be obtained from}
\PY{l+s+sd}{`G` by deletions and contractions; see Part 5. }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}
\PY{k+kn}{def}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{IsMinor}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{X}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{SimpleGraph}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{W}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{SimpleGraph}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{V}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k+kt}{Prop}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n}{Nonempty}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{X}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Model}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{o}{)}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\says $X$ is a minor of $G$ (“$G$ has an $X$-minor”) iff there is an $X$-model in $G$.

\how A model is \emph{data} (it lives in \lean{Type}), but “is a minor” should be a proposition.
\lean{Nonempty α} is the proposition “there is an element of \lean{α}”. To use
\lean{h : X.IsMinor G} we write \lean{obtain ⟨M⟩ := h}, which gives a model \lean{M} (allowed
whenever the goal is a proposition); to prove it we write \lean{⟨M⟩} for a model \lean{M}.

\decl{\lean{induce\_\allowbreak{}singleton\_\allowbreak{}connected}}{theorem}{induce\_singleton\_connected}
\begin{leancode}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},numbers=left,firstnumber=444]
\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ A single vertex induces a connected graph. We use Mathlib\PYZsq{}s characterisation}
\PY{l+s+sd}{`connected\PYZus{}iff\PYZus{}exists\PYZus{}forall\PYZus{}reachable`: a graph is connected iff some vertex `v` can reach}
\PY{l+s+sd}{every vertex `w`. }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}
\PY{k+kn}{theorem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{induce\PYZus{}singleton\PYZus{}connected}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{a}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{V}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{G}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{induce}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{a}\PY{o}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{o}{)}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Connected}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{by}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nf}{rw}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{[}\PY{n}{connected\PYZus{}iff\PYZus{}exists\PYZus{}forall\PYZus{}reachable}\PY{o}{]}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nf}{refine}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{a}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Set}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{mem\PYZus{}singleton}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{a}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{?}\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZus{}}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nf}{rintro}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{b}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hb}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nf}{rw}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{[}\PY{n}{Set}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{mem\PYZus{}singleton\PYZus{}iff}\PY{o}{]}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{at}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hb}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nf}{subst}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hb}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Reachable}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{refl}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZus{}}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\says A single vertex induces a connected graph.

\how
\begin{enumerate}
\item \lean{rw [connected\_\allowbreak{}iff\_\allowbreak{}exists\_\allowbreak{}forall\_\allowbreak{}reachable]}: by this Mathlib lemma, a graph is
  connected iff some vertex reaches every vertex. The goal becomes
  \lean{∃ v, ∀ w, (G.induce \{a\}).Reachable v w}.
\item \lean{refine ⟨⟨a, Set.mem\_\allowbreak{}singleton a⟩, ?\_\allowbreak{}⟩} chooses \lean{v = a} (as a vertex of
  $G[\{a\}]$, so together with the proof \lean{a ∈ \{a\}}).
\item \lean{rintro ⟨b, hb⟩}: an arbitrary vertex \lean{w}, split into \lean{b} and
  \lean{hb : b ∈ \{a\}}.
\item \lean{rw [Set.mem\_\allowbreak{}singleton\_\allowbreak{}iff] at hb} gives \lean{hb : b = a}, and \lean{subst hb} replaces
  \lean{b} by \lean{a} everywhere.
\item \lean{exact Reachable.refl \_\allowbreak{}}: every vertex reaches itself. (The two proofs of \lean{a ∈ \{a\}}
  inside the vertices are equal by proof irrelevance.)
\end{enumerate}

\tacs \lean{rw} with an \lean{↔} on the goal; \lean{refine} with an \lean{∃}-witness;
\lean{subst h} for \lean{h : b = a} where \lean{b} is a local variable; \lean{Reachable.refl}.

\decl{\lean{section minors}}{setup}{section minors}
\begin{leancode}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},numbers=left,firstnumber=455]
\PY{k+kn}{section}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{minors}

\PY{k+kn}{variable}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{G}\PY{o}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{X}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{SimpleGraph}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{W}\PY{o}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{X\PYZsq{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{SimpleGraph}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{U}\PY{o}{\PYZcb{}}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
Inside this section \lean{G} is implicit and \lean{X}, \lean{X'} are graphs on the types \lean{W} and
\lean{U}. Declarations preceded by \lean{variable (G) in} take \lean{G} explicitly after all.

\decl{\lean{isMinor\_\allowbreak{}of\_\allowbreak{}copy} (Remark 1.1 (1))}{theorem}{isMinor\_of\_copy (Remark 1.1 (1))}
\begin{leancode}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},numbers=left,firstnumber=459]
\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ **Remark 1.1 (1)**, general form. A *copy* of `X` in `G` (Mathlib: `X.Copy G`) is an}
\PY{l+s+sd}{injective graph homomorphism `f : X →g G`, i.e. a subgraph of `G` isomorphic to `X`. Every copy}
\PY{l+s+sd}{gives an `X`}\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{model: take the singletons `V\PYZus{}x = \PYZob{}f x\PYZcb{}` as branch sets. }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}
\PY{k+kn}{theorem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{isMinor\PYZus{}of\PYZus{}copy}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{f}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{X}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Copy}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{X}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{IsMinor}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{by}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nf}{refine}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{k}{fun}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{↦}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{f}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{o}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{fun}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{↦}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{f}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Set}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{mem\PYZus{}singleton}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZus{}}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{?}\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZus{}}\PY{o}{,}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{k}{fun}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{↦}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{induce\PYZus{}singleton\PYZus{}connected}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{f}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{o}{)}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{?}\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZus{}}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nb+bp}{·}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{c+c1}{\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{} `\PYZob{}f x\PYZcb{}` and `\PYZob{}f y\PYZcb{}` are disjoint for `x ≠ y`, because `f` is injective (Lecture 6)}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nf}{intro}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{y}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hxy}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nf}{rw}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{[}\PY{n}{Set}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{disjoint\PYZus{}singleton}\PY{o}{]}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nf}{intro}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hxy}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{f}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{injective}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{o}{)}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nb+bp}{·}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{c+c1}{\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{} an edge `xy` of `X` is mapped to the edge `f x f y` of `G`}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nf}{intro}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{y}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hxy}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{f}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Set}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{mem\PYZus{}singleton}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZus{}}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{f}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{y}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Set}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{mem\PYZus{}singleton}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZus{}}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{f}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{toHom}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{map\PYZus{}adj}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hxy}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\says If $G$ contains a copy of $X$ (an injective homomorphism $f : X \to G$, Mathlib's
\lean{X.Copy G}), then $X$ is a minor of $G$. The branch sets are the singletons $\{f(x)\}$.

\how \lean{refine ⟨⟨B, nonempty, ?\_\allowbreak{}, connected, ?\_\allowbreak{}⟩⟩}: the outer brackets build the
\lean{Nonempty}, the inner ones the \lean{Model} (fields in order). Three fields are given at once:
\begin{itemize}
\item \lean{branch := fun x ↦ \{f x\}};
\item \lean{nonempty}: \lean{⟨f x, Set.mem\_\allowbreak{}singleton \_\allowbreak{}⟩}, since \lean{f x ∈ \{f x\}};
\item \lean{connected}: the previous theorem.
\end{itemize}
The two holes \lean{?\_\allowbreak{}} become the two goals handled by the bullets:
\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{disjoint}: \lean{rw [Set.disjoint\_\allowbreak{}singleton]} turns \lean{Disjoint \{f x\} \{f y\}} into
  \lean{f x ≠ f y}; \lean{intro h} assumes \lean{f x = f y}, and \lean{f.injective h : x = y}
  contradicts \lean{hxy : x ≠ y}.
\item \emph{adjacency}: for an edge \lean{xy} of \lean{X}, the vertices \lean{f x ∈ \{f x\}} and
  \lean{f y ∈ \{f y\}} are adjacent because \lean{f} is a homomorphism:
  \lean{f.toHom.map\_\allowbreak{}adj hxy : G.Adj (f x) (f y)}. The flattened anonymous constructor
  \lean{⟨f x, \_\allowbreak{}, f y, \_\allowbreak{}, \_\allowbreak{}⟩} proves \lean{∃ a ∈ \{f x\}, ∃ b ∈ \{f y\}, G.Adj a b}.
\end{itemize}

\tacs \lean{refine} with a structure and holes; \lean{fun x ↦ ...} for the fields that are
functions; \lean{rw} with \lean{Set.disjoint\_\allowbreak{}singleton}; \lean{f.injective}; \lean{f.toHom.map\_\allowbreak{}adj}.

\decl{\lean{IsContained.\allowbreak{}isMinor}}{theorem}{IsContained.isMinor}
\begin{leancode}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},numbers=left,firstnumber=474]
\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ Remark 1.1 (1): if `G` contains a subgraph isomorphic to `X` (Mathlib: `X ⊑ G`), then `G`}
\PY{l+s+sd}{has an `X`}\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{minor. }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}
\PY{k+kn}{theorem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{IsContained}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{isMinor}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{X}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⊑}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{X}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{IsMinor}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n}{h}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{elim}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{fun}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{f}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{↦}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{isMinor\PYZus{}of\PYZus{}copy}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{f}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\says If $G$ contains a subgraph isomorphic to $X$ (\lean{X ⊑ G}), then $X$ is a minor of $G$.

\how In Mathlib \lean{X ⊑ G} is (an abbreviation for) \lean{Nonempty (X.Copy G)}.
\lean{Nonempty.elim : Nonempty α → (α → p) → p} uses the element to prove a proposition \lean{p};
so \lean{h.elim fun f ↦ isMinor\_\allowbreak{}of\_\allowbreak{}copy f} says “take a copy \lean{f} and apply the previous theorem”.
Because of the name, we may later write \lean{h.isMinor} for \lean{h : X ⊑ G}.

\decl{\lean{isMinor\_\allowbreak{}refl}, \lean{isMinor\_\allowbreak{}of\_\allowbreak{}le}, \lean{isMinor\_\allowbreak{}induce}}{theorems}{isMinor\_refl, isMinor\_of\_le, isMinor\_induce}
\begin{leancode}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},numbers=left,firstnumber=479]
\PY{k+kn}{variable}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{G}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{in}
\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ `G` is a minor of itself (Remark 1.1 (1), \PYZdq{}including `G` itself\PYZdq{}). }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}
\PY{k+kn}{theorem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{isMinor\PYZus{}refl}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{IsMinor}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n}{isMinor\PYZus{}of\PYZus{}copy}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{Copy}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{ofLE}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{le\PYZus{}rfl}\PY{o}{)}

\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ A subgraph `H ≤ G` is a minor of `G`. }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}
\PY{k+kn}{theorem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{isMinor\PYZus{}of\PYZus{}le}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{H}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{SimpleGraph}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{V}\PY{o}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{H}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{≤}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{H}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{IsMinor}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n}{isMinor\PYZus{}of\PYZus{}copy}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{Copy}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{ofLE}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{H}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{o}{)}

\PY{k+kn}{variable}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{G}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{in}
\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ An induced subgraph `G[s]` is a minor of `G`. }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}
\PY{k+kn}{theorem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{isMinor\PYZus{}induce}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{s}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Set}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{V}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{G}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{induce}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{s}\PY{o}{)}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{IsMinor}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n}{isMinor\PYZus{}of\PYZus{}copy}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{Copy}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{induce}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{s}\PY{o}{)}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\says $G$ is a minor of itself; a subgraph $H \le G$ is a minor of $G$; an induced subgraph $G[s]$
is a minor of $G$.

\how All three are one line: produce a copy and apply \lean{isMinor\_\allowbreak{}of\_\allowbreak{}copy}.
\lean{Copy.ofLE H G h} is the copy of \lean{H} in \lean{G} given by the identity map (\lean{h : H ≤ G});
for \lean{isMinor\_\allowbreak{}refl}, \lean{le\_\allowbreak{}rfl : G ≤ G}. \lean{Copy.induce G s} is the inclusion
$G[s] \to G$. \lean{variable (G) in} makes \lean{G} explicit for the next declaration only, so that
we can write \lean{isMinor\_\allowbreak{}refl G}.

\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ Vertex deletion: `G }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ v` is a minor of `G`. }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}
\PY{k+kn}{theorem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{isMinor\PYZus{}deleteVert}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{v}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{V}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{G}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{deleteVert}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{v}\PY{o}{)}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{IsMinor}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n}{isMinor\PYZus{}induce}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{w}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{|}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{w}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{≠}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{v}\PY{o}{\PYZcb{}}

\PY{k+kn}{variable}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{G}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{in}
\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ Edge deletion: `G \PYZbs{} s` is a minor of `G`. }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}
\PY{k+kn}{theorem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{isMinor\PYZus{}deleteEdges}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{s}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Set}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{Sym2}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{V}\PY{o}{)}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{G}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{deleteEdges}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{s}\PY{o}{)}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{IsMinor}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n}{isMinor\PYZus{}of\PYZus{}le}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{deleteEdges\PYZus{}le}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{s}\PY{o}{)}

\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ An isomorphic copy is a minor. }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}
\PY{k+kn}{theorem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Iso}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{isMinor}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{e}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{X}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{≃}\PY{n}{g}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{X}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{IsMinor}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n}{isMinor\PYZus{}of\PYZus{}copy}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{e}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{toCopy}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\says $G - v$, $G \setminus s$ and every graph isomorphic to $G$ are minors of $G$.

\how $G - v$ \emph{is} \lean{G.induce \{w | w ≠ v\}} by definition, so \lean{isMinor\_\allowbreak{}induce} applies
directly. \lean{deleteEdges\_\allowbreak{}le s : G.deleteEdges s ≤ G} (Mathlib) gives the second.
An isomorphism \lean{e : X ≃g G} is in particular an injective homomorphism, \lean{e.toCopy}.

\decl{\lean{isMinor\_\allowbreak{}contract}}{theorem}{isMinor\_contract}
\begin{leancode}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},numbers=left,firstnumber=507]
\PY{k+kn}{variable}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{G}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{in}
\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ Edge contraction: `G }\PY{l+s+sd}{/}\PY{l+s+sd}{ uv` is a minor of `G`. The branch set of the new vertex `u` is}
\PY{l+s+sd}{`\PYZob{}u, v\PYZcb{}`; every other vertex `a` has the branch set `\PYZob{}a\PYZcb{}`. }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}
\PY{k+kn}{theorem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{isMinor\PYZus{}contract}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{u}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{v}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{V}\PY{o}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{huv}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Adj}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{u}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{v}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{G}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{contract}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{u}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{v}\PY{o}{)}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{IsMinor}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{by}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nf}{refine}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{k}{fun}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{a}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{↦}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{w}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{|}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{w}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{a}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{1}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∨}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{a}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{1}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{u}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∧}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{w}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{v}\PY{o}{)}\PY{o}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{?}\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZus{}}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{?}\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZus{}}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{?}\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZus{}}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{?}\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZus{}}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nb+bp}{·}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{c+c1}{\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{} nonempty: `a` itself lies in its branch set}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nf}{intro}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{a}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{a}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{1}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Or}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{inl}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{rfl}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\says If $uv$ is an edge of $G$, then $G / uv$ is a minor of $G$.

\idea The branch set of the vertex \lean{a} of $G / uv$ is
\lean{\{w | w = a.1 ∨ (a.1 = u ∧ w = v)\}}: this is $\{u, v\}$ if $a = u$ and $\{a\}$ otherwise.

\how \lean{refine ⟨⟨branch, ?\_\allowbreak{}, ?\_\allowbreak{}, ?\_\allowbreak{}, ?\_\allowbreak{}⟩⟩} leaves the four conditions as goals.
\emph{Nonempty}: \lean{a.1} itself lies in its branch set; membership in \lean{\{w | P w\}} is by
definition \lean{P a.1}, here \lean{Or.inl rfl : a.1 = a.1 ∨ ...}.

\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nb+bp}{·}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{a}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{2}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{hw}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{symm}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{trans}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hw\PYZsq{}}\PY{o}{)}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nb+bp}{·}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{b}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{2}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{hw\PYZsq{}}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{symm}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{trans}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hw}\PY{o}{)}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nb+bp}{·}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hab}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{Subtype}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n+nf}{ext}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{hau}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{trans}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hbu}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{symm}\PY{o}{)}\PY{o}{)}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\how \emph{Disjoint.} \lean{rw [Set.disjoint\_\allowbreak{}left]} turns the goal into
\lean{∀ w, w ∈ B a → w ∉ B b}. \lean{rintro w (hw | ⟨hau, hw⟩) (hw' | ⟨hbu, hw'⟩)} introduces
\lean{w}, then the two membership hypotheses, splitting each \lean{∨}: four goals, each \lean{False}
(\lean{w ∉ B b} is \lean{w ∈ B b → False}).
\begin{itemize}
\item \lean{w = a.1} and \lean{w = b.1}: then \lean{a.1 = b.1} (\lean{hw.symm.trans hw'}), so
  \lean{a = b} by \lean{Subtype.ext}, contradicting \lean{hab : a ≠ b}.
\item \lean{w = a.1} and \lean{w = v}: then \lean{a.1 = v}, contradicting \lean{a.2 : a.1 ≠ v}.
\item \lean{w = v} and \lean{w = b.1}: likewise with \lean{b.2}.
\item \lean{a.1 = u} and \lean{b.1 = u}: then \lean{a.1 = b.1}, contradiction as in the first case.
\end{itemize}

\how \emph{Connected.} \lean{show} restates the goal in a definitionally equal but more readable
form (\lean{M.connected a} for the anonymous model unfolds to this). Then \lean{by\_\allowbreak{}cases hau : a.1 = u}.
\begin{itemize}
\item If \lean{a.1 = u}, the branch set equals \lean{\{u, v\}} (\lean{hset}). To prove an equality of
  sets, \lean{ext w} reduces it to \lean{w ∈ ... ↔ w ∈ ...}. The \lean{rw} unfolds membership in
  set-builder notation (\lean{Set.mem\_\allowbreak{}ofPred\_\allowbreak{}eq : (a ∈ \{x | p x\}) = p a}), in
  \lean{\{u, v\}} (\lean{Set.mem\_\allowbreak{}insert\_\allowbreak{}iff}, \lean{Set.mem\_\allowbreak{}singleton\_\allowbreak{}iff}), and replaces
  \lean{a.1} by \lean{u} (\lean{hau}). The goal is now
  \lean{w = u ∨ u = u ∧ w = v ↔ w = u ∨ w = v}, done by \lean{constructor} and \lean{rintro} in each
  direction. Finally \lean{rw [hset]} and Mathlib's \lean{induce\_\allowbreak{}pair\_\allowbreak{}connected\_\allowbreak{}of\_\allowbreak{}adj huv}:
  the two ends of an edge induce a connected graph. This is the only place where \lean{huv} is used.
\item Otherwise the branch set is \lean{\{a.1\}}: the second alternative \lean{a.1 = u ∧ w = v} is
  impossible, which is \lean{absurd h hau} (from \lean{h : P} and \lean{hau : ¬ P} conclude
  anything). Then \lean{induce\_\allowbreak{}singleton\_\allowbreak{}connected}.
\end{itemize}

\begin{leancode}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},numbers=left,firstnumber=550]
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nb+bp}{·}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{c+c1}{\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{} adjacency: unfold the three cases in the definition of `G / uv`}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nf}{rintro}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{a}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{b}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{hab}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{|}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{hau}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{|}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{hbu}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nb+bp}{·}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{a}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{1}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Or}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{inl}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{rfl}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{b}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{1}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Or}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{inl}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{rfl}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nb+bp}{·}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{v}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Or}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{inr}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{hau}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{rfl}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{b}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{1}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Or}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{inl}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{rfl}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nb+bp}{·}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{a}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{1}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Or}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{inl}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{rfl}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{v}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Or}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{inr}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{hbu}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{rfl}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\how \emph{Adjacency.} An edge of $G / uv$ between \lean{a} and \lean{b} is, by definition,
\lean{a.1 ≠ b.1 ∧ (case 1 ∨ case 2 ∨ case 3)}, and \lean{rintro} takes it apart directly. In each
case we name two vertices in the branch sets and the edge of $G$ between them:
case 1 uses the edge \lean{a.1 b.1}; case 2 (\lean{a.1 = u}, \lean{G.Adj v b.1}) uses
\lean{v ∈ B a} (because \lean{a.1 = u}); case 3 uses \lean{v ∈ B b}.

\tacs \lean{refine}, \lean{rw [Set.disjoint\_\allowbreak{}left]}, \lean{rintro} with \lean{(p | q)} patterns,
\lean{Subtype.ext}, \lean{.symm.trans}, \lean{show}, \lean{by\_\allowbreak{}cases}, \lean{ext} for sets,
\lean{absurd}, flattened anonymous constructors.

\decl{Walks and reachability}{commands}{Walks and reachability}
\begin{leancode}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},numbers=left,firstnumber=564]
\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZsh{}}\PY{n}{check}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{@}\PY{n}{SimpleGraph}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Walk}
\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZsh{}}\PY{n}{check}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{@}\PY{n}{SimpleGraph}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Walk}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{support}
\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZsh{}}\PY{n}{check}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{@}\PY{n}{SimpleGraph}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Reachable}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
A walk \lean{p : G.Walk a b} is a sequence of vertices from \lean{a} to \lean{b}, consecutive ones
adjacent. Walks are built like lists: \lean{Walk.nil : G.Walk a a} and
\lean{Walk.cons (h : G.Adj a m) (p : G.Walk m b) : G.Walk a b}. \lean{p.support} is the list of its
vertices, and \lean{G.Reachable a b} is \lean{Nonempty (G.Walk a b)}. Lean prints:
\begin{leansays}
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
@Walk : {V : Type u_4} → SimpleGraph V → V → V → Type u_4
@Walk.support : {V : Type u_4} → {G : SimpleGraph V} → {u v : V} → G.Walk u v → List V
@Reachable : {V : Type u_4} → SimpleGraph V → V → V → Prop
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leansays}
Because walks are built from \lean{nil} and \lean{cons}, we can prove statements about all walks
by \lean{induction}, just as for natural numbers or lists.

\decl{\lean{reachable\_\allowbreak{}induce\_\allowbreak{}of\_\allowbreak{}walk}}{theorem}{reachable\_induce\_of\_walk}
