\PY{+w}{      }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{List}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{mem\PYZus{}cons\PYZus{}of\PYZus{}mem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZus{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{p}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{start\PYZus{}mem\PYZus{}support}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{k}{have}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h1}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{G}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{induce}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{s}\PY{o}{)}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Adj}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{x}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ha}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{m}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hm}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h1}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{reachable}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{trans}
\PY{+w}{      }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{ih}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{k}{fun}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{z}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hz}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{↦}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hp}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{z}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{k}{by}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{rw}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{[}\PY{n}{Walk}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{support\PYZus{}cons}\PY{o}{]}\PY{n+nb+bp}{;}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{List}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{mem\PYZus{}cons\PYZus{}of\PYZus{}mem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZus{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hz}\PY{o}{)}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hm}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hb}\PY{o}{)}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\says If all vertices of a walk from $a$ to $b$ in $G$ lie in $s$, then $a$ and $b$ are reachable
from each other in $G[s]$.

\how Induction on the walk \lean{p}.
\begin{itemize}
\item \lean{nil}: the walk has length zero, so \lean{a = b}, and \lean{Reachable.refl}.
\item \lean{@cons x m y h p ih}: the walk is \lean{x}, then the edge \lean{h : G.Adj x m}, then a walk
  \lean{p} from \lean{m} to \lean{y}. The \lean{@} lets us name all arguments of \lean{cons},
  including the implicit vertices \lean{x m y}. Lean automatically generalises the hypotheses
  \lean{hp}, \lean{ha}, \lean{hb} that mention the walk, so the induction hypothesis is
  \lean{ih : (∀ z ∈ p.support, z ∈ s) → ∀ (hm : m ∈ s) (hb : y ∈ s), (G.induce s).Reachable ⟨m, hm⟩ ⟨y, hb⟩}.
  \begin{enumerate}
  \item \lean{hm : m ∈ s}: \lean{apply hp m} reduces it to \lean{m ∈ (cons h p).support};
    \lean{Walk.support\_\allowbreak{}cons} says this support is \lean{x :: p.support}, and \lean{m} is in
    \lean{p.support} (its start, \lean{p.start\_\allowbreak{}mem\_\allowbreak{}support}), hence in the longer list
    (\lean{List.mem\_\allowbreak{}cons\_\allowbreak{}of\_\allowbreak{}mem}).
  \item \lean{h1}: the edge \lean{xm} is an edge of $G[s]$. The proof is just \lean{h}, because
    adjacency in $G[s]$ is adjacency in $G$ by definition.
  \item \lean{h1.reachable.trans (ih ...)}: \lean{x} reaches \lean{m} by one edge, and \lean{m} reaches
    \lean{y} by the induction hypothesis (whose first argument, “all vertices of \lean{p} lie in
    \lean{s}”, is proved inline by \lean{by rw ...; exact ...}).
  \end{enumerate}
\end{itemize}

\tacs \lean{induction p with | nil => ... | @cons ... => ...} (structural induction with named
cases); \lean{apply}; \lean{rw [Walk.support\_\allowbreak{}cons]}; \lean{Adj.reachable}, \lean{Reachable.trans};
\lean{by tac₁; tac₂} runs two tactics in a term position.

\decl{\lean{Reachable.\allowbreak{}induce\_\allowbreak{}mono}}{theorem}{Reachable.induce\_mono}
\begin{leancode}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},numbers=left,firstnumber=583]
\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ If `s ⊆ t`, then vertices reachable in `G[s]` are reachable in `G[t]`. }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}
\PY{k+kn}{theorem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Reachable}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{induce\PYZus{}mono}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{s}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{t}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Set}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{V}\PY{o}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{hst}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{s}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⊆}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{t}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{a}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{b}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{V}\PY{o}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{ha}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{a}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∈}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{s}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{hb}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{b}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∈}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{s}\PY{o}{)}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{G}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{induce}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{s}\PY{o}{)}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Reachable}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{a}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ha}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{b}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hb}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{G}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{induce}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{t}\PY{o}{)}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Reachable}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{a}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hst}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ha}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{b}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hst}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hb}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n}{h}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{map}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{G}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{induceHomOfLE}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hst}\PY{o}{)}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{toHom}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\says If $s \subseteq t$ and $a, b$ are reachable from each other in $G[s]$, then also in $G[t]$.

\how Mathlib's \lean{G.induceHomOfLE hst : G.induce s ↪g G.induce t} is the inclusion of $G[s]$
into $G[t]$ (a graph embedding); \lean{.toHom} forgets that it is injective. A homomorphism maps
walks to walks, so \lean{Reachable.map f h} maps reachability along \lean{f}; the image of
\lean{⟨a, ha⟩} is \lean{⟨a, hst ha⟩} by definition.

\decl{\lean{IsMinor.\allowbreak{}trans}: a minor of a minor is a minor}{theorem}{IsMinor.trans: a minor of a minor is a minor}
\begin{leancode}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},numbers=left,firstnumber=589]
\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ A minor of a minor is a minor. If `X` has a model in `H` with branch sets `B₁ x ⊆ V(H)`}

\PY{l+s+sd}{`B x = ⋃ (h ∈ B₁ x), B₂ h` are the branch sets of a model of `X` in `G`. }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}

\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n}{X}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{IsMinor}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{by}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{k}{obtain}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{M}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hXH}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{k}{obtain}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{N}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hHG}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{k}{let}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{B}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{W}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{→}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Set}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{V}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{fun}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{↦}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{a}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{|}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∃}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∈}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{M}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{branch}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{a}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∈}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{N}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{branch}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{o}{\PYZcb{}}

\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nf}{refine}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{B}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{?}\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZus{}}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{?}\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZus{}}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{?}\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZus{}}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{?}\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZus{}}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\says If $X$ is a minor of $H$ and $H$ is a minor of $G$, then $X$ is a minor of $G$.

\idea If $X$ has a model in $H$ with branch sets $B_1(x) \subseteq V(H)$ and $H$ has a model in $G$
with branch sets $B_2(h) \subseteq V(G)$, then $B(x) = \bigcup_{h \in B_1(x)} B_2(h)$ are the
branch sets of an $X$-model in $G$.

\how \lean{obtain ⟨M⟩ := hXH} and \lean{obtain ⟨N⟩ := hHG} unpack the two \lean{Nonempty}s into
models \lean{M : X.Model H} and \lean{N : H.Model G}. \lean{let B := ...} defines the new branch sets
as a local definition (\lean{a ∈ B x} unfolds to \lean{∃ h ∈ M.branch x, a ∈ N.branch h}).
\lean{hsub} records that $B_2(h) \subseteq B(x)$ for $h \in B_1(x)$; as \lean{s ⊆ t} means
\lean{∀ a, a ∈ s → a ∈ t}, its proof is the function \lean{fun x h hh a ha ↦ ⟨h, hh, ha⟩}.
\lean{refine ⟨⟨B, ?\_\allowbreak{}, ?\_\allowbreak{}, ?\_\allowbreak{}, ?\_\allowbreak{}⟩⟩} leaves four goals.

\PY{+w}{    }\PY{k}{obtain}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{h}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hh}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{M}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{nonempty}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{k}{obtain}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{a}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ha}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{N}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{nonempty}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}

\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nf}{rw}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{[}\PY{n}{Set}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{disjoint\PYZus{}left}\PY{o}{]}

\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nb+bp}{·}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{subst}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hhh\PYZsq{}}
\PY{+w}{      }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Set}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{disjoint\PYZus{}left}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{1}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{M}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{disjoint}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{y}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hxy}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hh}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hh\PYZsq{}}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nb+bp}{·}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Set}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{disjoint\PYZus{}left}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{1}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{N}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{disjoint}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h\PYZsq{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hhh\PYZsq{}}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ha}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ha\PYZsq{}}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\how \emph{Nonempty}: pick \lean{h ∈ B₁ x} and then \lean{a ∈ B₂ h}; then \lean{a ∈ B x} is witnessed
by \lean{⟨h, hh, ha⟩}.
\emph{Disjoint}: let \lean{a ∈ B x ∩ B y}, so \lean{a ∈ B₂ h} with \lean{h ∈ B₁ x} and
\lean{a ∈ B₂ h'} with \lean{h' ∈ B₁ y}. \lean{by\_\allowbreak{}cases hhh' : h = h'}. If \lean{h = h'},
\lean{subst} replaces \lean{h'} by \lean{h}, and \lean{h} lies in both \lean{B₁ x} and \lean{B₁ y},
contradicting \lean{M.disjoint}. Otherwise \lean{a} lies in both \lean{B₂ h} and \lean{B₂ h'},
contradicting \lean{N.disjoint}. \lean{Set.disjoint\_\allowbreak{}left.1} is the left-to-right direction of
the \lean{↔}: from \lean{Disjoint s t} get \lean{∀ a ∈ s, a ∉ t}.

\how \emph{Connected} --- the main step. After rewriting with
\lean{connected\_\allowbreak{}iff\_\allowbreak{}exists\_\allowbreak{}forall\_\allowbreak{}reachable} we need one vertex \lean{a₀ ∈ B x} that reaches all
others. The \lean{key} claim is proved by induction on a walk \lean{q} in $H[B_1(x)]$ from
\lean{h₁} to \lean{h₂}: \emph{every} \lean{a₁ ∈ B₂ h₁} reaches \emph{every} \lean{a₂ ∈ B₂ h₂} in
$G[B(x)]$. (The \lean{a₁}, \lean{a₂} are quantified \emph{after} \lean{q}, so the induction
hypothesis is general enough.)
\begin{itemize}
\item \lean{nil} (\lean{h₁ = h₂ = h}): \lean{a₁} and \lean{a₂} lie in the same branch set \lean{B₂ h},
  which is connected (\lean{N.connected h}, used as a function of two vertices); transfer from
  $G[B_2(h)]$ to $G[B(x)]$ with \lean{Reachable.induce\_\allowbreak{}mono}.
\item \lean{cons} (an edge \lean{hadj} from \lean{h₁} to \lean{h'}, then a walk \lean{q} from \lean{h'}):
  \lean{N.adj} gives an edge \lean{b₁ b'} of $G$ with \lean{b₁ ∈ B₂ h₁} and \lean{b' ∈ B₂ h'}. Then
  \lean{a₁ → b₁} inside \lean{B₂ h₁} (\lean{r1}), \lean{b₁ → b'} along the edge (\lean{r2}), and
  \lean{b' → a₂} by the induction hypothesis \lean{ih b' hb' a₂ ha₂}. Chain them with \lean{.trans}.
\end{itemize}

\how To finish \emph{connected}: take \lean{a₀} as the base vertex. For an arbitrary vertex
\lean{⟨a, h, hh, ha⟩} of $G[B(x)]$ (the pattern splits its membership proof), $B_1(x)$ is
connected in $H$, so there is a walk \lean{q} from \lean{h₀} to \lean{h} (\lean{obtain ⟨q⟩} takes it
out of the \lean{Nonempty} that \lean{Reachable} is), and \lean{key} finishes.
\emph{Adjacency}: an edge \lean{xy} of \lean{X} gives an edge \lean{hh'} of \lean{H} between
\lean{B₁ x} and \lean{B₁ y} (\lean{M.adj}), which gives an edge \lean{aa'} of \lean{G} between
\lean{B₂ h ⊆ B x} and \lean{B₂ h' ⊆ B y} (\lean{N.adj}).

\tacs \lean{obtain ⟨M⟩ := h} (unpack \lean{Nonempty}); \lean{let}; \lean{refine} with four holes;
\lean{by\_\allowbreak{}cases} + \lean{subst}; \lean{Set.disjoint\_\allowbreak{}left.1}; \lean{have key : ∀ ..., ... := by ...}
followed by \lean{induction q with}; \lean{Reachable.trans}; \lean{Adj.reachable}.

\says Being a minor only depends on $X$ and on $G$ up to isomorphism.

\how If \lean{e : X ≃g X'}, then \lean{e.isMinor : X.IsMinor X'}, and transitivity gives
\lean{X.IsMinor G} from \lean{X'.IsMinor G}. Similarly on the right. For the \lean{↔}, the anonymous
constructor \lean{⟨mp, mpr⟩} takes the two directions, using \lean{e} and its inverse \lean{e.symm}.
Note the dot notation \lean{e.isMinor.trans h}: it is \lean{IsMinor.trans (Iso.isMinor e) h}.

\PY{+w}{      }\PY{n+nf}{rw}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{[}\PY{n}{hxy}\PY{o}{]}
\PY{+w}{      }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hf}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{y}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Set}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{disjoint\PYZus{}left}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{1}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{M}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{disjoint}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{y}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hne}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h1}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h2}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Fintype}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{card\PYZus{}le\PYZus{}of\PYZus{}injective}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{f}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hinj}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\says A minor has at most as many vertices as the graph: $|V(X)| \le |V(G)|$.

\how Choose one vertex in each branch set: \lean{f x := (M.nonempty x).some}. (\lean{Set.Nonempty.some}
picks an element of a nonempty set, using the axiom of choice; \lean{some\_\allowbreak{}mem} says it lies in the
set.) This \lean{f : W → V} is injective: if \lean{f x = f y} but \lean{x ≠ y} (\lean{by\_\allowbreak{}contra hne}),
then \lean{f x} lies in \lean{M.branch x} and (after \lean{rw [hxy]}) in \lean{M.branch y}, which are
disjoint. Finally \lean{Fintype.card\_\allowbreak{}le\_\allowbreak{}of\_\allowbreak{}injective}: an injection between finite types gives
\lean{card W ≤ card V}. The assumptions \lean{[Fintype W] [Fintype V]} say that both vertex types
are finite, so that \lean{Fintype.card} makes sense.

\tacs \lean{let}, \lean{.some}/\lean{.some\_\allowbreak{}mem} (choice), \lean{by\_\allowbreak{}contra} (assume the negation of
the goal), \lean{rw [hxy]} to move from \lean{f x} to \lean{f y}.

\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n}{Fin}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{castSucc\PYZus{}injective}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{l+m+mi}{4}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}

\end{leancode}
\says $K_5$ is not a minor of $K_4$; $K_{3,3}$ is not a minor of $K_5$; $K_4$ is a minor of $K_5$.

\how The first two: \lean{intro h} (to prove \lean{¬ P}, assume \lean{P} and derive \lean{False});
\lean{have h' := h.card\_\allowbreak{}le} gives \lean{Fintype.card (Fin 5) ≤ Fintype.card (Fin 4)};
\lean{rw [Fintype.card\_\allowbreak{}fin, Fintype.card\_\allowbreak{}fin] at h'} computes both sides, \lean{h' : 5 ≤ 4};
\lean{omega} sees the contradiction. For $K_{3,3}$ the lemmas \lean{card\_\allowbreak{}vertices\_\allowbreak{}K33} and
\lean{card\_\allowbreak{}vertices\_\allowbreak{}K5} from Part 1 give \lean{6 ≤ 5}.

The third is a copy of $K_4$ in $K_5$: \lean{Fin.castSucc : Fin 4 → Fin 5} sends \lean{i} to
\lean{i}. The inner \lean{⟨Fin.castSucc, \_\allowbreak{}⟩} is a graph homomorphism \lean{K₄ →g K₅}; its second
component proves “adjacent goes to adjacent”, i.e.\ \lean{i ≠ j → castSucc i ≠ castSucc j}: from
\lean{h : i ≠ j} and \lean{h' : castSucc i = castSucc j} we get \lean{h (Fin.castSucc\_\allowbreak{}injective 4 h')}.
The outer pair adds injectivity, making a \lean{Copy}.

\tacs \lean{intro} for \lean{¬}, \lean{have h' := ...}, \lean{rw ... at}, \lean{omega} (linear
arithmetic over \lean{ℕ}), anonymous constructors for structures.


% =====================================================================
\section{Part 5: minors as sequences of operations (Proposition 1.4)}

The notes define: a minor of $G$ is a graph obtained from $G$ by a sequence of vertex deletions,
edge deletions and edge contractions, in any order; “$G$ has an $X$-minor” means that some graph
in such a sequence is isomorphic to $X$. Part 5 formalises this definition and proves that it is
equivalent to the model definition of Part 4 (Proposition 1.4), in both directions.

\PY{+w}{      }\PY{n}{IsMinorOp}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{X}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{H}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{→}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{IsMinorOp}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{H}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{→}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{IsMinorOp}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{X}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\says \lean{IsMinorOp X G} (“$X$ is obtained from $G$ by operations”) holds iff it can be derived
with these six rules: do nothing; delete the vertices outside a set \lean{s} (giving
\lean{G.induce s}); delete a set of edges; contract an edge; rename vertices along an isomorphism;
do one sequence after another.

\how An \lean{inductive} predicate is defined by its \emph{rules} (constructors), and it is true
exactly when it can be derived by finitely many applications of the rules. Two consequences:
\begin{itemize}
\item to \emph{prove} \lean{IsMinorOp X G}, apply rules, e.g.\ \lean{IsMinorOp.contract G huv};
\item to \emph{use} \lean{h : IsMinorOp X G}, do \lean{induction h}: it suffices to check that the
  property you want is preserved by each rule.
\end{itemize}
Deleting a set of vertices (or edges) at once is the same as deleting them one at a time
(Remark 1.2), so the rules \lean{induce} and \lean{deleteEdges} are just convenient.

The type \lean{\{α : Type u\} → SimpleGraph α → \{β : Type u\} → SimpleGraph β → Prop} says that
\lean{IsMinorOp} relates a graph on some type \lean{α} to a graph on some type \lean{β}. The
operations change the vertex type (\lean{V} becomes \lean{↥s} or \lean{\{w // w ≠ v\}}), and all
these types must live in one fixed universe \lean{Type u}, hence the explicit \lean{universe u}.

\decl{\lean{IsMinorOp.\allowbreak{}deleteVert}, \lean{IsMinorOp.\allowbreak{}deleteEdge}}{theorems}{IsMinorOp.deleteVert, IsMinorOp.deleteEdge}
\begin{leancode}

\says If $X$ is obtained from $G$ by operations, then $X$ is a minor of $G$ in the sense of models.

\how \lean{induction h with}, one line per rule. In each case Lean gives the arguments of the rule
and, for the premises that are themselves \lean{IsMinorOp} statements, an induction hypothesis:
\begin{itemize}
\item \lean{refl}, \lean{induce}, \lean{deleteEdges}, \lean{contract}: these are exactly the
  theorems \lean{isMinor\_\allowbreak{}refl}, \lean{isMinor\_\allowbreak{}induce}, \lean{isMinor\_\allowbreak{}deleteEdges},
  \lean{isMinor\_\allowbreak{}contract} of Part 4.
\item \lean{iso e \_\allowbreak{} ih}: the premise \lean{IsMinorOp X' G} is not needed (\lean{\_\allowbreak{}}), but its induction
  hypothesis \lean{ih : X'.IsMinor G} is; then \lean{ih.of\_\allowbreak{}iso\_\allowbreak{}left e}.
\item \lean{trans \_\allowbreak{} \_\allowbreak{} ih₁ ih₂}: \lean{ih₁ : X.IsMinor H}, \lean{ih₂ : H.IsMinor G}, and
  \lean{ih₁.trans ih₂}.
\end{itemize}

\tacs \lean{induction h with | rule args => ...} on an inductive predicate.

\decl{The converse: Steps A, B, C}{overview}{The converse: Steps A, B, C}
Let $V_x$ be the branch sets of an $X$-model in a \emph{finite} graph $G$. We remove superfluous
vertices of $G$ one at a time:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Step A.} A vertex $v$ in no branch set is deleted; the branch sets are still a model
  in $G - v$ (\lean{Model.deleteVert}).
\item \textbf{Step B.} If some $V_x$ has two vertices, then $G[V_x]$ (connected) has an edge $ac$;
  contract it. The branch sets, with $c$ removed, are a model in $G / ac$ (\lean{Model.contract}).
\item \textbf{Step C.} If every vertex lies in a branch set and all branch sets are single vertices
  $\{f(x)\}$, then $f$ is a bijection with $xy \in E(X) \Rightarrow f(x)f(y) \in E(G)$, so $X$ is
  isomorphic to $G$ minus the edges not of the form $f(x)f(y)$ (\lean{Model.isMinorOp\_\allowbreak{}of\_\allowbreak{}singletons}).
\end{itemize}
Steps A and B decrease the number of vertices, so induction on $|V(G)|$ finishes
(\lean{IsMinor.isMinorOp}). The file opens \lean{section converse} with \lean{variable \{G\} \{X : SimpleGraph W\}}.

\says If the vertex $v$ lies in no branch set, then the same branch sets form an $X$-model in $G - v$.

\how This \lean{def} builds a new model, field by field (\lean{where}); a field that is a function may
take its arguments before \lean{:=} (\lean{nonempty x := ...}).
\begin{itemize}
\item \lean{branch x := \{w | w.1 ∈ M.branch x\}}: the vertices of $G - v$ whose underlying vertex is
  in $V_x$.
\item \lean{nonempty}: take \lean{a ∈ V\_\allowbreak{}x}; it is a vertex of $G - v$ because \lean{a ≠ v}:
  if \lean{h : a = v}, then \lean{h ▸ ha : v ∈ M.branch x} (\lean{▸} rewrites the type of \lean{ha}
  using \lean{h}), contradicting \lean{hv x}.
\item \lean{disjoint}: membership \lean{w ∈ \{w | w.1 ∈ M.branch x\}} is by definition
  \lean{w.1 ∈ M.branch x}, so the old disjointness applies directly.
\item \lean{connected}: the map \lean{f : a ↦ ⟨⟨a.1, \_\allowbreak{}⟩, a.2⟩} is a graph homomorphism
  $G[V_x] \to (G - v)[V_x]$ (adjacency is the same on both sides by definition, so the proof of
  “preserves adjacency” is \lean{fun h ↦ h}), and it is surjective. Mathlib's
  \lean{Connected.map f hf hG}: a surjective homomorphic image of a connected graph is connected.
  With dot notation, \lean{(M.connected x).map f ?\_\allowbreak{}} leaves the surjectivity as the goal: given a
  vertex \lean{⟨⟨a, hav⟩, ha⟩}, it is the image of \lean{⟨a, ha⟩} (\lean{rfl}).
\item \lean{adj}: the edge \lean{ab} of $G$ between $V_x$ and $V_y$ also lies in $G - v$.
\end{itemize}

\tacs \lean{where} with field arguments, \lean{h ▸ e}, \lean{let f : A →g B := ⟨map, proof⟩},
\lean{Connected.map}, \lean{refine ... ?\_\allowbreak{}}.

\says If $a$ and $c$ are adjacent vertices in the same branch set $V_x$, then the branch sets (with
$c$ removed) form an $X$-model in $G / ac$. (Recall: in $G / ac$ the vertex $a$ survives and $c$ is
deleted.)

\how \lean{branch y := \{w | w.1 ∈ M.branch y\}} as in Step A.
\emph{Nonempty}: take \lean{d ∈ V\_\allowbreak{}y}. If \lean{d = c}, then \lean{V\_\allowbreak{}y} contains \lean{c}, so
\lean{y = x} (\lean{eq\_\allowbreak{}of\_\allowbreak{}mem\_\allowbreak{}of\_\allowbreak{}mem}), and after \lean{rw [hyx]} the vertex \lean{a ≠ c} of
\lean{V\_\allowbreak{}x} is a witness. Otherwise \lean{d} itself survives. \emph{Disjoint}: as in Step A.

\how \emph{Connected, the case \lean{y = x}} (the branch set that loses $c$).
\lean{classical} allows \lean{if ... then ... else} on any proposition (here \lean{p.1 = c}, and
\lean{V} has no decidable equality otherwise). The map \lean{f} sends a vertex \lean{p} of $G[V_x]$
to the same vertex of $(G / ac)[V_x \setminus \{c\}]$, except that \lean{c} goes to \lean{a}.
\lean{hadj} shows that \lean{f} maps edges to reachable pairs. The lemmas
\lean{dite\_\allowbreak{}eq\_\allowbreak{}left hpc} and \lean{dite\_\allowbreak{}eq\_\allowbreak{}right hpc} evaluate the \lean{if}: they say
\lean{(if h : P then t h else e h) = t hpc} when \lean{hpc : P}, resp.\ \lean{= e hpc} when
\lean{hpc : ¬ P}. Four cases:
\begin{itemize}
\item \lean{p = c} and \lean{q = c}: impossible, because adjacent vertices are different.
\item \lean{p = c}, \lean{q ≠ c}: \lean{f p = a}, \lean{f q = q}. If \lean{q = a}, both images are the
  same vertex (\lean{e3}, by \lean{Subtype.ext} twice, one for each layer of subtype), and after
  \lean{rw [e3]} the goal \lean{Reachable z z} is closed automatically: \lean{rw} tries \lean{rfl},
  and \lean{Reachable.refl} is registered as a \lean{@[refl]} lemma. If \lean{q ≠ a}, then \lean{a} and
  \lean{q} are adjacent in $G / ac$ by the second case of the definition (\lean{a} inherits the
  neighbour \lean{q} of \lean{c}; \lean{hpc ▸ hpq'} turns \lean{G.Adj p.1 q.1} into \lean{G.Adj c q.1}).
\item \lean{p ≠ c}, \lean{q = c}: symmetric, using the third case of the definition.
\item neither is \lean{c}: the edge survives (\lean{contract\_\allowbreak{}adj\_\allowbreak{}of\_\allowbreak{}adj}).
\end{itemize}

\how \lean{hwalk} extends \lean{hadj} from edges to walks, by \lean{induction w}: the empty walk is
\lean{Reachable.refl}; a walk \lean{cons h w'} gives \lean{(hadj \_\allowbreak{} \_\allowbreak{} h).trans ih}. Finally the
vertex \lean{a} reaches every vertex \lean{⟨⟨q, hqc⟩, hq⟩} of $(G/ac)[V_x \setminus \{c\}]$:
\lean{rw [← e1, ← e2]} rewrites both as images under \lean{f}, $G[V_x]$ contains a walk \lean{w}
from \lean{a} to \lean{q}, and \lean{hwalk} applies.

\how \emph{Adjacency.} An edge \lean{yz} of \lean{X} gives an edge \lean{pq} of $G$ with
\lean{p ∈ V\_\allowbreak{}y}, \lean{q ∈ V\_\allowbreak{}z}.
\begin{itemize}
\item If \lean{p = c}: then \lean{y = x}, and we replace \lean{p} by \lean{a}. We need \lean{q ≠ c}
  (\lean{q} is adjacent to \lean{c}) and \lean{a ≠ q} (otherwise \lean{q ∈ V\_\allowbreak{}x}, so \lean{z = x = y},
  but \lean{X} has no loops: \lean{X.ne\_\allowbreak{}of\_\allowbreak{}adj hyz}). The edge \lean{aq} of $G / ac$ is the second
  case of the definition. \lean{hyx ▸ ha} turns \lean{a ∈ M.branch x} into \lean{a ∈ M.branch y}.
\item If \lean{q = c}: symmetric (third case of the definition).
\item Otherwise the edge \lean{pq} itself survives.
\end{itemize}

\tacs \lean{classical}; \lean{if h : P then ... else ...} with \lean{dite\_\allowbreak{}eq\_\allowbreak{}left}/\lean{dite\_\allowbreak{}eq\_\allowbreak{}right};
nested \lean{by\_\allowbreak{}cases}; \lean{absurd}; \lean{rw [e1, e2]} with local equations; \lean{▸};
\lean{Subtype.ext}; \lean{induction} on walks; \lean{Connected.map}.

\says If every vertex of $G$ lies in a branch set (\lean{hcover}) and every branch set has only one
vertex (\lean{hsingle}: any two of its vertices are equal), then \lean{IsMinorOp X G}.

\how The theorem is stated outside \lean{section converse}, with explicit universe-\lean{u} types
\lean{α}, \lean{β}, because the conclusion is \lean{IsMinorOp}.
\begin{itemize}
\item \lean{f x := (M.nonempty x).some} picks the vertex of $V_x$; \lean{g v := (hcover v).choose}
  picks a vertex of $X$ whose branch set contains \lean{v} (\lean{Exists.choose} uses choice;
  \lean{choose\_\allowbreak{}spec} is its property, here \lean{hg}).
\item \lean{e : α ≃ β} is a bijection (\lean{Equiv}) built with structure-instance notation
  \lean{\{ toFun := f, invFun := g, left\_\allowbreak{}inv := ..., right\_\allowbreak{}inv := ... \}}.
  \lean{left\_\allowbreak{}inv}: \lean{g (f x) = x}, because \lean{f x} lies in the branch sets of both
  \lean{g (f x)} and \lean{x}. \lean{right\_\allowbreak{}inv}: \lean{f (g v) = v}, because both lie in the single-vertex
  branch set of \lean{g v}.
\end{itemize}

\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\how
\begin{itemize}
\item \lean{hadj}: an edge \lean{xy} of \lean{X} gives an edge \lean{ab} of \lean{G} with
  \lean{a ∈ V\_\allowbreak{}x}, \lean{b ∈ V\_\allowbreak{}y}; since the branch sets are singletons, \lean{a = f x} and
  \lean{b = f y} (the \lean{rw ... at hab}).
\item \lean{S} is the set of edges of \lean{G} that do \emph{not} come from an edge of \lean{X}.
\item \lean{iso : X ≃g G.deleteEdges S}. The syntax \lean{\{ e with map\_\allowbreak{}rel\_\allowbreak{}iff' := ... \}} takes all
  fields of \lean{e} (the bijection) and adds the missing field. After \lean{intro x y},
  \lean{change} states the goal readably: \lean{(G.deleteEdges S).Adj (f x) (f y) ↔ X.Adj x y}.
  \begin{itemize}
  \item ($\Rightarrow$) We have \lean{hS : s(f x, f y) ∉ S}. Suppose \lean{¬ X.Adj x y}
    (\lean{by\_\allowbreak{}contra hxy}). \lean{apply hS} changes the goal \lean{False} to
    \lean{s(f x, f y) ∈ S}, i.e.\ \lean{¬ ∃ x' y', X.Adj x' y' ∧ s(f x, f y) = s(f x', f y')}.
    So \lean{rintro ⟨x', y', hxy', he⟩}. By \lean{Sym2.eq\_\allowbreak{}iff}, an equation of unordered pairs
    \lean{s(a, b) = s(c, d)} means \lean{(a = c ∧ b = d) ∨ (a = d ∧ b = c)}. By injectivity of
    \lean{f}, in the first case \lean{x = x'}, \lean{y = y'}, contradicting \lean{hxy}; in the second
    \lean{x = y'}, \lean{y = x'}, contradicting \lean{hxy} by symmetry.
  \item ($\Leftarrow$) \lean{hadj} gives the edge of \lean{G}, and it is not in \lean{S}, because it
    \emph{does} come from the edge \lean{xy}: \lean{hS ⟨x, y, hxy, rfl⟩}.
  \end{itemize}
\item Finally: \lean{G.deleteEdges S} is obtained from \lean{G} by an operation, and \lean{X} is
  isomorphic to it: \lean{IsMinorOp.iso iso (IsMinorOp.deleteEdges G S)}.
\end{itemize}

\tacs \lean{classical}; \lean{.some}, \lean{.choose}/\lean{.choose\_\allowbreak{}spec}; \lean{Equiv} as a structure
instance; \lean{\{ e with ... \}}; \lean{change}; \lean{by\_\allowbreak{}contra}; \lean{apply} to a negation;
\lean{rw [Sym2.eq\_\allowbreak{}iff] at he}; \lean{rcases} with \lean{|}; \lean{rw [hinj h1, hinj h2] at hxy}.

\says For a finite graph $G$ (\lean{[Fintype β]}), every $X$-model in $G$ gives a sequence of
operations producing $X$.

\how
\begin{enumerate}
\item \lean{obtain ⟨M⟩ := h} gets the model.
\item \lean{suffices key : ∀ n β [Fintype β] G, card β ≤ n → X.Model G → IsMinorOp X G from ...}:
  it is enough to prove the more general statement \lean{key}, for \emph{all} finite graphs with at
  most \lean{n} vertices; the part after \lean{from} shows how \lean{key} gives the original goal
  (take \lean{n = card β}, \lean{le\_\allowbreak{}rfl}). The generalisation is necessary because Steps A and B
  change the graph \emph{and its vertex type}.
\item \lean{induction n using Nat.strong\_\allowbreak{}induction\_\allowbreak{}on with | \_\allowbreak{} n ih} is \emph{strong} induction:
  the hypothesis \lean{ih} holds for all \lean{m < n}. Lean shows (later in the proof)
\begin{leansays}
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
ih : ∀ m < n, ∀ (β : Type u) [inst : Fintype β] (G : SimpleGraph β),
       Fintype.card β ≤ m → ∀ (a : X.Model G), X.IsMinorOp G
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leansays}
\item \lean{intro β \_\allowbreak{} G hcard N}: the \lean{\_\allowbreak{}} introduces the \lean{Fintype} instance without a name.
\item \textbf{Step A}, \lean{by\_\allowbreak{}cases hdel : ∃ v, ∀ x, v ∉ N.branch x}. If such \lean{v} exists:
  \begin{itemize}
  \item \lean{hlt}: $G - v$ has fewer vertices. Mathlib's \lean{Fintype.card\_\allowbreak{}subtype\_\allowbreak{}lt} says
    \lean{card \{w // p w\} < card β} if \lean{¬ p x} for some \lean{x}; here \lean{p w := w ≠ v},
    \lean{x := v} (a \emph{named argument}), and \lean{¬ (v ≠ v)} is \lean{fun h ↦ h rfl}.
  \item \lean{have := ih (card β - 1) (by omega) ... (by omega) (N.deleteVert v hv)} applies the
    induction hypothesis to $G - v$ with its model from Step A. The two \lean{omega}s prove
    \lean{card β - 1 < n} and \lean{card \{w // w ≠ v\} ≤ card β - 1} from \lean{hcard} and
    \lean{hlt} (\lean{omega} treats the cardinalities as unknown natural numbers).
    \lean{have :=} without a name calls the result \lean{this}.
  \item \lean{this.trans (IsMinorOp.deleteVert G v)}: $X$ from $G - v$, and $G - v$ from $G$.
  \end{itemize}
\end{enumerate}

\PY{+w}{        }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{N}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{contract}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ha}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hc\PYZsq{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hadj}\PY{o}{)}
\PY{+w}{      }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{this}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{trans}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{IsMinorOp}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{contract}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hadj}\PY{o}{)}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nb+bp}{·}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{c+c1}{\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{} Step C: all branch sets are single vertices and cover `G`}
\PY{+w}{      }\PY{n+nf}{push}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Not}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{at}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hcon}
\PY{+w}{      }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{N}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{isMinorOp\PYZus{}of\PYZus{}singletons}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hdel}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hcon}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\how
\begin{enumerate}\setcounter{enumi}{5}
\item \lean{push Not at hdel} moves the negation inside: \lean{¬ ∃ v, ∀ x, v ∉ N.branch x} becomes
\begin{leansays}
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
hdel : ∀ (v : β), ∃ x, v ∈ N.branch x
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leansays}
\item \textbf{Step B}, \lean{by\_\allowbreak{}cases hcon : ∃ x, ∃ a ∈ V\_\allowbreak{}x, ∃ c ∈ V\_\allowbreak{}x, a ≠ c}. If some branch set
  has two vertices \lean{a ≠ c}, we first find a \emph{neighbour} \lean{c'} of \lean{a} in \lean{V\_\allowbreak{}x}.
  The syntax \lean{obtain ⟨c', hc', hadj⟩ : ∃ c' ∈ N.branch x, G.Adj a c' := by ...} states a claim,
  proves it, and destructures it. Proof of the claim: take a walk \lean{w} from \lean{a} to \lean{c}
  in $G[V_x]$. Mathlib's \lean{exists\_\allowbreak{}boundary\_\allowbreak{}dart}: a walk that starts inside a set \lean{S} and
  ends outside it uses a \emph{dart} (a directed edge) \lean{d} with \lean{d.fst ∈ S} and
  \lean{d.snd ∉ S}. With \lean{S = \{⟨a, ha⟩\}} we get \lean{d.fst = a}, so \lean{d.snd} is a neighbour
  of \lean{a} in \lean{V\_\allowbreak{}x}. (The third argument proves \lean{c ∉ \{a\}} from \lean{hac : a ≠ c}.)
  Then, as in Step A, \lean{hlt}, the induction hypothesis for $G / ac'$ with the model
  \lean{N.contract ha hc' hadj}, and \lean{IsMinorOp.contract G hadj}.
\item \textbf{Step C}. Otherwise \lean{push Not at hcon} gives
\begin{leansays}
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
hcon : ∀ (x : α), ∀ a ∈ N.branch x, ∀ c ∈ N.branch x, a = c
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leansays}
  which, together with \lean{hdel}, is exactly the hypothesis of \lean{isMinorOp\_\allowbreak{}of\_\allowbreak{}singletons}.
\end{enumerate}

\tacs \lean{suffices ... from}; \lean{induction n using Nat.strong\_\allowbreak{}induction\_\allowbreak{}on}; \lean{intro} with
\lean{\_\allowbreak{}}; named arguments \lean{(x := v)}; \lean{have :=} and \lean{this}; \lean{omega};
\lean{push Not at h}; \lean{obtain pattern : statement := by proof}; \lean{exists\_\allowbreak{}boundary\_\allowbreak{}dart}.

\decl{\lean{isMinor\_\allowbreak{}iff\_\allowbreak{}isMinorOp} (Proposition 1.4)}{theorem}{isMinor\_iff\_isMinorOp (Proposition 1.4)}
\begin{leancode}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},numbers=left,firstnumber=992]
\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ **Proposition 1.4.** For a finite graph `G`, `G` has an `X`}\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{minor (there is an `X`}\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{model in}
\PY{l+s+sd}{`G`) if and only if `X` can be obtained from `G` by deletions and contractions. }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}
\PY{k+kn}{theorem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{isMinor\PYZus{}iff\PYZus{}isMinorOp}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{α}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{β}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k+kt}{Type}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{u}\PY{o}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{[}\PY{n}{Fintype}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{β}\PY{o}{]}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{X}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{SimpleGraph}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{α}\PY{o}{\PYZcb{}}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{o}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{SimpleGraph}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{β}\PY{o}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{X}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{IsMinor}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{↔}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{IsMinorOp}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{X}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{k}{fun}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{↦}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{isMinorOp}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{fun}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{↦}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{isMinor}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\says For finite $G$: $X$ is a minor of $G$ (via models) iff $X$ is obtained from $G$ by deletions
and contractions (up to isomorphism).
\how The anonymous constructor \lean{⟨mp, mpr⟩} of an \lean{↔}, with the two directions just proved.

% =====================================================================
\section{Part 6: subdivisions (Remark 1.1 (2))}

A \emph{subdivision} of $X$ replaces every edge $xy$ of $X$ by a path from $x$ to $y$; different
paths meet only in their ends, and no path passes through a vertex of $X$. “$G$ contains an
$X$-subdivision” means that $G$ has such a subgraph; the images of the vertices of $X$ are the
\emph{branch vertices}. The interior of the path $P_{xy}$ consists of its vertices other than the
two ends.

\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ `G` contains an `X`}\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{subdivision. }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}
\PY{k+kn}{def}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{HasSubdivision}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{X}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{SimpleGraph}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{W}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{SimpleGraph}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{V}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k+kt}{Prop}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n}{Nonempty}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{X}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Subdivision}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{o}{)}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\says An $X$-subdivision in $G$ consists of
\begin{itemize}
\item \lean{vert : W → V}, the branch vertices, \lean{injective};
\item for every edge \lean{xy} of \lean{X}, a walk \lean{path x y h} in \lean{G} from \lean{vert x} to
  \lean{vert y}, which is a path (\lean{isPath}: no vertex repeated);
\item \lean{path\_\allowbreak{}symm}: \lean{path y x} is \lean{path x y} backwards (the path belongs to the
  \emph{unordered} edge);
\item \lean{interior\_\allowbreak{}ne\_\allowbreak{}vert}: an interior vertex \lean{z} of a path (\lean{z} on the path,
  \lean{z ≠ vert x}, \lean{z ≠ vert y}) is not a branch vertex;
\item \lean{interior\_\allowbreak{}disjoint}: for two different edges (\lean{s(x, y) ≠ s(x', y')}), an interior vertex
  of the first path is not on the second path at all.
\end{itemize}
\lean{HasSubdivision X G} is \lean{Nonempty (X.Subdivision G)}, as for \lean{IsMinor}.

\tacs \lean{p.support} is the list of vertices of the walk \lean{p}; \lean{p.reverse} is the walk
backwards; \lean{p.IsPath} says the walk visits no vertex twice.

\begin{leancode}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},numbers=left,firstnumber=1038]

\PY{l+s+sd}{(Lecture 5: the fields of a structure can be given one by one with `where`.) }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}

\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nf}{rw}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{[}\PY{n}{Walk}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{support\PYZus{}cons}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Walk}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{support\PYZus{}nil}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{List}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{mem\PYZus{}cons}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{List}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{mem\PYZus{}singleton}\PY{o}{]}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{at}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hz}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nf}{rcases}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hz}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{with}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hz}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{|}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hz}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nb+bp}{·}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hzx}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hz}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nb+bp}{·}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hzy}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hz}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\says A copy of $X$ in $G$ is an $X$-subdivision in which every path has length one.

\how Field by field:
\begin{itemize}
\item \lean{vert := f}, \lean{injective := f.injective}.
\item \lean{path x y h := Walk.cons (f.toHom.map\_\allowbreak{}adj h) Walk.nil}: one edge, then stop.
\item \lean{isPath}: \lean{Walk.IsPath.nil.cons} is the rule “if \lean{p} is a path and
  \lean{u ∉ p.support}, then \lean{cons h p} is a path”, applied to the empty path. \lean{apply} leaves
  the goal \lean{f x ∉ Walk.nil.support}; \lean{rw [Walk.support\_\allowbreak{}nil, List.mem\_\allowbreak{}singleton]} turns it into
  \lean{¬ f x = f y} (the support of the empty walk at \lean{f y} is the list \lean{[f y]}), and an edge
  joins different vertices.
\item \lean{path\_\allowbreak{}symm := rfl}: reversing a one-edge walk computes to the one-edge walk in the other
  direction (the two adjacency proofs are equal by proof irrelevance).
\item \lean{interior\_\allowbreak{}ne\_\allowbreak{}vert}, \lean{interior\_\allowbreak{}disjoint}: a one-edge path has no interior vertex.
  \lean{exfalso} changes the goal to \lean{False}; rewriting the support to \lean{[f x, f y]} turns
  \lean{hz} into \lean{z = f x ∨ z = f y}, and both cases contradict \lean{hzx}, \lean{hzy}.
\end{itemize}

\tacs \lean{apply} with a partially applied rule; \lean{rw [Walk.support\_\allowbreak{}nil, ...]};
\lean{exfalso} (prove \lean{False} instead of the goal); \lean{rcases hz with hz | hz}.

\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n}{h}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{elim}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{fun}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{f}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{↦}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{f}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{toSubdivision}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}

\PY{k+kn}{variable}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{G}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{in}
\PY{k+kn}{theorem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hasSubdivision\PYZus{}refl}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{HasSubdivision}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{Copy}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{ofLE}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{le\PYZus{}rfl}\PY{o}{)}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{toSubdivision}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\says If $X \sqsubseteq G$ then $G$ contains an $X$-subdivision; $G$ contains a $G$-subdivision.
\how As in Part 4: \lean{h.elim} takes a copy out of the \lean{Nonempty}, and \lean{⟨f.toSubdivision⟩}
puts the subdivision back into a \lean{Nonempty}.

\decl{\lean{not\_\allowbreak{}mem\_\allowbreak{}support\_\allowbreak{}takeUntil\_\allowbreak{}of\_\allowbreak{}isPath}}{theorem}{not\_mem\_support\_takeUntil\_of\_isPath}
\begin{leancode}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},numbers=left,firstnumber=1072]
\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ On a path `p` from `a` to `b` and a vertex `z ≠ b` of `p`, the initial segment}
\PY{l+s+sd}{`p.takeUntil z` of `p` ending at `z` does not contain `b`. (A path visits no vertex twice.) }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}
\PY{k+kn}{theorem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{not\PYZus{}mem\PYZus{}support\PYZus{}takeUntil\PYZus{}of\PYZus{}isPath}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{[}\PY{n}{DecidableEq}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{V}\PY{o}{]}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{a}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{b}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{V}\PY{o}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{p}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Walk}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{a}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{b}\PY{o}{\PYZcb{}}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{hp}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{p}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{IsPath}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{z}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{V}\PY{o}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{hz}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{z}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∈}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{p}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{support}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{hzb}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{z}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{≠}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{b}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n}{b}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∉}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{p}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{takeUntil}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{z}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hz}\PY{o}{)}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{support}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{by}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nf}{intro}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hb}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{c+c1}{\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{} `p = (p.takeUntil z) ++ (p.dropUntil z)`, and the vertices of a path are pairwise distinct}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{k}{have}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hnodup}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{p}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{takeUntil}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{z}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hz}\PY{o}{)}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{append}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{p}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{dropUntil}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{z}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hz}\PY{o}{)}\PY{o}{)}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{support}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Nodup}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{by}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nf}{rw}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{[}\PY{n}{Walk}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{take\PYZus{}spec}\PY{o}{]}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hp}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{support\PYZus{}nodup}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nf}{rw}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{[}\PY{n}{Walk}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{support\PYZus{}append}\PY{o}{]}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{at}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hnodup}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{c+c1}{\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{} `b` is the last vertex of `p.dropUntil z`, and `b ≠ z` is not its first vertex}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{k}{have}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hb\PYZsq{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{b}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∈}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{p}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{dropUntil}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{z}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hz}\PY{o}{)}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{support}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{tail}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{by}

\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nf}{rw}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{[}\PY{n+nb+bp}{←}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Walk}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{cons\PYZus{}tail\PYZus{}support}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{List}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{mem\PYZus{}cons}\PY{o}{]}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{at}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hend}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nf}{rcases}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hend}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{with}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{|}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nb+bp}{·}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{absurd}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hzb}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{symm}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nb+bp}{·}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{List}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{disjoint\PYZus{}of\PYZus{}nodup\PYZus{}append}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hnodup}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hb}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hb\PYZsq{}}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\says Let $p$ be a path from $a$ to $b$ and $z \ne b$ a vertex of $p$. Then $b$ is not on the initial
segment \lean{p.takeUntil z} of $p$ (from $a$ to $z$).

\how \lean{[DecidableEq V]} is needed because \lean{takeUntil} has to search the walk for \lean{z}.
\begin{enumerate}
\item \lean{intro hb}: assume \lean{b} is on the initial segment; goal \lean{False}.
\item \lean{hnodup}: \lean{p} is \lean{(p.takeUntil z) ++ (p.dropUntil z)} (\lean{Walk.take\_\allowbreak{}spec}), and
  the support of a path has no duplicates (\lean{hp.support\_\allowbreak{}nodup}).
\item \lean{rw [Walk.support\_\allowbreak{}append] at hnodup}: the support of \lean{p ++ q} is
  \lean{p.support ++ q.support.tail} (the common vertex \lean{z} is counted once).
\item \lean{hb'}: \lean{b} is the last vertex of \lean{p.dropUntil z}, and it is not the first one
  (that is \lean{z ≠ b}), so it is in the tail of the support. After
  \lean{rw [← Walk.cons\_\allowbreak{}tail\_\allowbreak{}support, List.mem\_\allowbreak{}cons] at hend} Lean shows
\begin{leansays}
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
hend : b = z ∨ b ∈ (p.dropUntil z hz).support.tail
⊢ b ∈ (p.dropUntil z hz).support.tail
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leansays}
  and the first case contradicts \lean{hzb} (\lean{absurd h hzb.symm}).
\item So \lean{b} is in both parts of a list without duplicates: \lean{List.disjoint\_\allowbreak{}of\_\allowbreak{}nodup\_\allowbreak{}append}
  gives the contradiction.
\end{enumerate}

\tacs \lean{rw [Walk.take\_\allowbreak{}spec]} inside a \lean{have}; \lean{rw [← lemma, ...] at h} (the
\lean{←} rewrites \lean{p.support} into \lean{a :: p.support.tail}); \lean{absurd}.

\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nf}{classical}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{c+c1}{\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{} Step 0: every edge `xy` of `X` is owned by one of its ends. We take the first component of}

\PY{+w}{  }\PY{k}{have}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{own\PYZus{}or}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∀}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{y}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{own}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{y}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∨}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{own}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{y}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{by}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nf}{intro}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{y}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{k}{have}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Sym2}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{out\PYZus{}fst\PYZus{}mem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{s}\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{x}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{y}\PY{o}{)}\PY{o}{)}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nf}{rw}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{[}\PY{n}{Sym2}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{mem\PYZus{}iff}\PY{o}{]}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{at}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nf}{rcases}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{with}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{|}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}

\says If $G$ contains an $X$-subdivision, then $G$ has an $X$-minor.

\idea Give each edge $xy$ of $X$ to one of its ends, its \emph{owner}. The branch set of $x$ is
$\{\mathrm{vert}(x)\}$ together with the interiors of all paths owned by $x$. These sets are
connected (unions of initial segments of paths starting at $\mathrm{vert}(x)$) and disjoint
(different paths have disjoint interiors), and the path $P_{xy}$ contains an edge between the branch
sets of $x$ and $y$: the first edge on which it leaves the branch set of the owner.

\how \textbf{Step 0: owners.} \lean{(s(x, y)).out} is a representative ordered pair of the unordered
pair (chosen once and for all by Mathlib, using choice); \lean{own x y} says its first component is
\lean{x}.
\begin{itemize}
\item \lean{own\_\allowbreak{}or}: \lean{Sym2.out\_\allowbreak{}fst\_\allowbreak{}mem} says the first component is \lean{x} or \lean{y}
  (\lean{Sym2.mem\_\allowbreak{}iff}). In the second case, \lean{right} chooses \lean{own y x}; \lean{change}
  unfolds it to \lean{(s(y, x)).out.1 = y}; \lean{rw [Sym2.eq\_\allowbreak{}swap]} turns \lean{s(y, x)} into
  \lean{s(x, y)}; and \lean{h} is exactly that.
\item \lean{own\_\allowbreak{}eq}: if \lean{s(x, y) = s(x', y')}, both owners are the first component of the same
  representative: \lean{congrArg (fun e ↦ e.out.1) he} and two \lean{trans}.
\end{itemize}

\how \textbf{Step 1: branch sets.} \lean{B x} is \lean{\{vert x\}} together with all \lean{z} that are
interior vertices of some path \lean{P x y} owned by \lean{x}. \lean{hvert}: \lean{vert x ∈ B x}.
\lean{hvert\_\allowbreak{}not}: \lean{vert x ∉ B x'} for \lean{x ≠ x'}: either \lean{vert x = vert x'} (impossible by
injectivity) or \lean{vert x} is an interior vertex of a path, which \lean{interior\_\allowbreak{}ne\_\allowbreak{}vert} forbids
(\lean{... x rfl} uses \lean{w := x} and \lean{rfl : vert x = vert x}). Then
\lean{refine ⟨⟨B, nonempty, ?\_\allowbreak{}, ?\_\allowbreak{}, ?\_\allowbreak{}⟩⟩}.

\how \textbf{Step 2: disjoint.} Let \lean{z ∈ B x ∩ B x'}.
If \lean{z = vert x}: \lean{hvert\_\allowbreak{}not}. Otherwise \lean{z} is an interior vertex of \lean{P x y}, owned
by \lean{x}. If \lean{z = vert x'}: \lean{interior\_\allowbreak{}ne\_\allowbreak{}vert}. Otherwise \lean{z} is also an interior
vertex of \lean{P x' y'}, owned by \lean{x'}. If the two edges are equal, their owners are equal
(\lean{own\_\allowbreak{}eq}), so \lean{x = x'}: contradiction. If they are different: \lean{interior\_\allowbreak{}disjoint}.

\PY{+w}{      }\PY{n+nb+bp}{·}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{have}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hwy}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{w}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{≠}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{S}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{vert}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{y}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{fun}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{↦}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hyP}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{▸}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hw}\PY{o}{)}
\PY{+w}{        }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Or}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{inr}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{y}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hy}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hown}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hwP}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hwx}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hwy}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}

\how \textbf{Step 3: connected.} Base vertex \lean{vert x}. If \lean{z = vert x}, \lean{subst} and
\lean{Reachable.refl}. If \lean{z} is an interior vertex of \lean{P x y}, walk along the initial segment
\lean{(P x y).takeUntil z} from \lean{vert x} to \lean{z}. \lean{apply reachable\_\allowbreak{}induce\_\allowbreak{}of\_\allowbreak{}walk}
(Part 4) leaves the goal “every vertex \lean{w} of this segment lies in \lean{B x}”. Such \lean{w} lies
on \lean{P x y} (\lean{support\_\allowbreak{}takeUntil\_\allowbreak{}subset\_\allowbreak{}support}). Either \lean{w = vert x}, or \lean{w} is an
interior vertex of \lean{P x y}: \lean{w ≠ vert y}, because \lean{vert y} is not on the initial segment
(\lean{hyP}, by the previous lemma).

\end{leancode}
\how \textbf{Step 4: adjacency.} First for an edge \lean{xy} owned by \lean{x} (\lean{key}). The path
\lean{P x y} starts in \lean{B x} (\lean{hvert x}) and ends outside (\lean{hy}). By
\lean{exists\_\allowbreak{}boundary\_\allowbreak{}dart} it has a dart \lean{d} leaving \lean{B x}; at that point Lean shows
\begin{leansays}
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
d : G.Dart
hd : d ∈ (S.path x y h).darts
hdx : d.toProd.1 ∈ B x
hdy : d.toProd.2 ∉ B x
⊢ ∃ a ∈ B x, ∃ b ∈ B y, G.Adj a b
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leansays}
(\lean{d.fst}/\lean{d.snd} are displayed as \lean{d.toProd.1}/\lean{d.toProd.2}).
\lean{refine ⟨d.fst, hdx, d.snd, ?\_\allowbreak{}, d.adj⟩} leaves \lean{d.snd ∈ B y}. Suppose not
(\lean{by\_\allowbreak{}contra hne}). \lean{apply hdy} changes the goal to \lean{d.snd ∈ B x}, and this holds: \lean{d.snd}
is on \lean{P x y}, it is not \lean{vert x} (that is in \lean{B x}), and not \lean{vert y} (that is in
\lean{B y}), so it is an interior vertex of a path owned by \lean{x}. In fact this shows
\lean{d.snd = vert y}.

For an arbitrary edge \lean{xy}, \lean{own\_\allowbreak{}or} says that \lean{x} or \lean{y} owns it; in the second case
apply \lean{key} to \lean{yx} and swap the two ends of the edge (\lean{G.adj\_\allowbreak{}symm}).

\tacs \lean{let} for a predicate; \lean{right}; \lean{change}; \lean{rw [Sym2.eq\_\allowbreak{}swap]}; \lean{congrArg};
\lean{rintro} with nested \lean{∃}-patterns; \lean{subst}; \lean{apply} with a lemma whose remaining
hypothesis becomes the goal; \lean{exists\_\allowbreak{}boundary\_\allowbreak{}dart}; \lean{by\_\allowbreak{}contra} + \lean{apply hdy}.

\decl{\lean{HasSubdivision.\allowbreak{}isMinor}}{theorem}{HasSubdivision.isMinor}
\begin{leancode}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},numbers=left,firstnumber=1180]
\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ Remark 1.1 (2): `G` contains an `X`}\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{subdivision → `G` has an `X`}\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{minor. }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}
\PY{k+kn}{theorem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{HasSubdivision}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{isMinor}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{X}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{HasSubdivision}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{X}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{IsMinor}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n}{h}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{elim}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{fun}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{S}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{↦}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{S}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{isMinor}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\says $G$ contains an $X$-subdivision $\Rightarrow$ $G$ has an $X$-minor.
\how Take the subdivision out of the \lean{Nonempty} (\lean{h.elim}) and apply \lean{Subdivision.isMinor}.

% =====================================================================
\section{Part 7: Propositions 1.5 and 1.6}

These propositions say that for $X = K_{3,3}$ and $X = K_5$ the converse of Remark 1.1 (2) holds.
Their proofs choose an $X$-model with as few vertices as possible and analyse the trees $H[V_x]$
(a tree with at most three leaves is a path or a subdivided $K_{1,3}$). This needs spanning
trees, leaves and a minimality argument, so the file states them and leaves the proofs as
\lean{sorry}. Lean accepts the file but warns “declaration uses \lean{sorry}”.

\decl{\lean{IsMinor.\allowbreak{}hasSubdivision\_\allowbreak{}of\_\allowbreak{}degree\_\allowbreak{}le\_\allowbreak{}three} (Prop. 1.5)}{theorem, sorry}{IsMinor.hasSubdivision\_of\_degree\_le\_three (Prop. 1.5)}
\begin{leancode}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},numbers=left,firstnumber=1199]
\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ **Proposition 1.5.** Let `X` be a graph with maximum degree at most three. If `G` has an}
\PY{l+s+sd}{`X`}\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{minor, then `G` contains an `X`}\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{subdivision.}

\PY{l+s+sd}{*Proof sketch* (notes, p. 3). Let `H` be an `X`}\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{model in `G` with as few vertices as possible,}
\PY{l+s+sd}{with branch sets `V\PYZus{}x`. For each `x`, let `T\PYZus{}x` be the tree obtained from `H[V\PYZus{}x]` by adding,}
\PY{l+s+sd}{for each neighbour `y` of `x` in `X`, the unique vertex of `V\PYZus{}y` adjacent to `V\PYZus{}x`. By}
\PY{l+s+sd}{minimality of `H`, `T\PYZus{}x` is a tree with exactly `d\PYZus{}X(x) ≤ 3` leaves, hence a path or a}
\PY{l+s+sd}{subdivision of `K₁,₃`. It follows that `H` is a subdivision of `X`. }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}
\PY{k+kn}{theorem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{IsMinor}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{hasSubdivision\PYZus{}of\PYZus{}degree\PYZus{}le\PYZus{}three}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{[}\PY{n}{Fintype}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{V}\PY{o}{]}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{[}\PY{n}{Fintype}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{W}\PY{o}{]}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{[}\PY{n}{DecidableRel}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{X}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Adj}\PY{o}{]}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{hX}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∀}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{X}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{degree}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{≤}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{l+m+mi}{3}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{X}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{IsMinor}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{X}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{HasSubdivision}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{by}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{g+gr}{sorry}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\says If $X$ has maximum degree at most three and $G$ has an $X$-minor, then $G$ contains an
$X$-subdivision.

\how Not proved (\lean{sorry}). The instance arguments \lean{[Fintype V] [Fintype W] [DecidableRel X.Adj]}
are needed to \emph{state} the theorem: \lean{X.degree x}, the number of neighbours of \lean{x},
only makes sense when the neighbour set is finite and adjacency can be decided.

\idea (Notes, p.~3.) Take an $X$-model $H$ in $G$ with as few vertices as possible. For each $x$, let
$T_x$ be $H[V_x]$ plus, for each neighbour $y$ of $x$, the unique vertex of $V_y$ adjacent to $V_x$.
By minimality $T_x$ is a tree with exactly $d_X(x) \le 3$ leaves, hence a path or a subdivided
$K_{1,3}$; so $H$ is a subdivision of $X$.

\decl{\lean{hasSubdivision\_\allowbreak{}or\_\allowbreak{}of\_\allowbreak{}K5\_\allowbreak{}isMinor} (Prop. 1.6)}{theorem, sorry}{hasSubdivision\_or\_of\_K5\_isMinor (Prop. 1.6)}
\begin{leancode}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},numbers=left,firstnumber=1211]
\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ **Proposition 1.6.** If `G` has a `K₅`}\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{minor, then `G` contains a `K₅`}\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ or a}
\PY{l+s+sd}{`K₃,₃`}\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{subdivision.}

\PY{l+s+sd}{*Proof sketch* (notes, p. 3). Let `H` be a minimal `K₅`}\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{model with branch sets `X₁, …, X₅`}
\PY{l+s+sd}{and trees `T₁, …, T₅` as in Proposition 1.5; each `T\PYZus{}i` has exactly four leaves. If every `T\PYZus{}i`}
\PY{l+s+sd}{has a vertex of degree four, `H` is a `K₅`}\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{subdivision. Otherwise some `T₁` has maximum degree}
\PY{l+s+sd}{three and is a subdivision of the \PYZdq{}split `K₁,₄`\PYZdq{}; contracting the other four branch sets to}
\PY{l+s+sd}{single vertices gives a minor of `H` containing a `K₃,₃`}\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{subdivision, so `G` has a}
\PY{l+s+sd}{`K₃,₃`}\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{minor, and by Proposition 1.5 (`K₃,₃` has maximum degree three) a `K₃,₃`}\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{subdivision. }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}
\PY{k+kn}{theorem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hasSubdivision\PYZus{}or\PYZus{}of\PYZus{}K5\PYZus{}isMinor}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{[}\PY{n}{Fintype}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{V}\PY{o}{]}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{K5}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{IsMinor}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n}{K5}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{HasSubdivision}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∨}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{K33}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{HasSubdivision}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{by}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{g+gr}{sorry}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\says If $G$ has a $K_5$-minor, then $G$ contains a $K_5$-subdivision or a $K_{3,3}$-subdivision.

\idea (Notes, p.~3.) Take a minimal $K_5$-model with trees $T_1, \dots, T_5$ as above; each has four
leaves. If every $T_i$ has a vertex of degree four, $H$ is a $K_5$-subdivision. Otherwise some $T_1$
has maximum degree three and is a subdivision of the “split $K_{1,4}$”; contracting the other four
branch sets gives a $K_{3,3}$-minor, and Proposition 1.5 turns it into a $K_{3,3}$-subdivision.

\tacs \lean{sorry} closes any goal without a proof. The statement can still be \emph{used} (Part 8),
but every theorem that uses it inherits the gap.

% =====================================================================
\section{Part 8: Kuratowski's theorem, minor version $\Leftrightarrow$ subdivision version}

\decl{\lean{not\_\allowbreak{}hasSubdivision\_\allowbreak{}of\_\allowbreak{}not\_\allowbreak{}isMinor}: (3) $\Rightarrow$ (2)}{theorem}{not\_hasSubdivision\_of\_not\_isMinor: (3) => (2)}
\begin{leancode}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},numbers=left,firstnumber=1236]
\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ Kuratowski\PYZsq{}s theorem, (3) → (2): if `G` has no `K₅`}\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ and no `K₃,₃`}\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{minor, then `G` contains}
\PY{l+s+sd}{no `K₅`}\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ and no `K₃,₃`}\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{subdivision. This direction uses only Remark 1.1 (2) and is proved}
\PY{l+s+sd}{completely. }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}
\PY{k+kn}{theorem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{not\PYZus{}hasSubdivision\PYZus{}of\PYZus{}not\PYZus{}isMinor}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{h5}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{¬}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{K5}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{IsMinor}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{h33}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{¬}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{K33}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{IsMinor}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nb+bp}{¬}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{K5}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{HasSubdivision}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∧}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{¬}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{K33}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{HasSubdivision}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{by}

\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nb+bp}{·}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{intro}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h5}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{isMinor}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nb+bp}{·}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{intro}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h33}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{isMinor}

\says If $G$ has no $K_5$-minor and no $K_{3,3}$-minor, then $G$ contains no $K_5$-subdivision and no
$K_{3,3}$-subdivision.

\how \lean{constructor} splits the \lean{∧} into two goals. Each is a \lean{¬}: \lean{intro h} assumes a
subdivision, \lean{h.isMinor} (Remark 1.1 (2), Part 6) turns it into a minor, and \lean{h5}/\lean{h33}
gives \lean{False}. This direction is completely proved (no \lean{sorry} involved).

\decl{\lean{not\_\allowbreak{}isMinor\_\allowbreak{}of\_\allowbreak{}not\_\allowbreak{}hasSubdivision}: (2) $\Rightarrow$ (3)}{theorem}{not\_isMinor\_of\_not\_hasSubdivision: (2) => (3)}
\begin{leancode}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},numbers=left,firstnumber=1247]

\PY{l+s+sd}{`G` has no `K₅`}\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ and no `K₃,₃`}\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{minor. This direction uses Propositions 1.5 and 1.6. }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}
\PY{k+kn}{theorem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{not\PYZus{}isMinor\PYZus{}of\PYZus{}not\PYZus{}hasSubdivision}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{[}\PY{n}{Fintype}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{V}\PY{o}{]}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{h5}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{¬}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{K5}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{HasSubdivision}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{o}{)}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{h33}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{¬}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{K33}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{HasSubdivision}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{¬}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{K5}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{IsMinor}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∧}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{¬}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{K33}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{IsMinor}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{by}

\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nf}{intro}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nf}{rcases}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hasSubdivision\PYZus{}or\PYZus{}of\PYZus{}K5\PYZus{}isMinor}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{with}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h\PYZsq{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{|}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h\PYZsq{}}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nb+bp}{·}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h5}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h\PYZsq{}}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nb+bp}{·}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h33}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h\PYZsq{}}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nb+bp}{·}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{c+c1}{\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{} a `K₃,₃`\PYZhy{}minor gives a `K₃,₃`\PYZhy{}subdivision (Proposition 1.5, as `Δ(K₃,₃) = 3`)}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nf}{intro}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h33}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{h}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{hasSubdivision\PYZus{}of\PYZus{}degree\PYZus{}le\PYZus{}three}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{K33\PYZus{}degree\PYZus{}le\PYZus{}three}\PY{o}{)}

\says If $G$ contains no $K_5$- and no $K_{3,3}$-subdivision, then $G$ has no $K_5$- and no
$K_{3,3}$-minor.

\how \lean{constructor}, then:
\begin{itemize}
\item a $K_5$-minor gives a $K_5$- or a $K_{3,3}$-subdivision (Proposition 1.6); \lean{rcases ... with h' | h'}
  splits the \lean{∨}, and each case contradicts a hypothesis;
\item a $K_{3,3}$-minor gives a $K_{3,3}$-subdivision by Proposition 1.5, because
  \lean{K33\_\allowbreak{}degree\_\allowbreak{}le\_\allowbreak{}three} (Part 1) says that $K_{3,3}$ has maximum degree three. The instance
  arguments of Proposition 1.5 for $K_{3,3}$ are found automatically (\lean{Fin 3 ⊕ Fin 3} is finite,
  and Part 1 declared \lean{instance : DecidableRel K33.Adj}).
\end{itemize}
This direction depends on the two \lean{sorry}s of Part 7.

\PY{l+s+sd}{`G` has no `K₅`}\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ or `K₃,₃`}\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{minor. }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}

\PY{+w}{      }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n+nb+bp}{¬}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{K5}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{IsMinor}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∧}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{¬}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{K33}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{IsMinor}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{by}

\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nb+bp}{·}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{rintro}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{h5}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h33}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{not\PYZus{}isMinor\PYZus{}of\PYZus{}not\PYZus{}hasSubdivision}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h5}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h33}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nb+bp}{·}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{rintro}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{h5}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h33}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}

\how \lean{constructor} splits the \lean{↔}; in each direction \lean{rintro ⟨h5, h33⟩} splits the
