\decl{\lean{kuratowski} (minor version)}{theorem}{kuratowski (minor version)}
\begin{leancode}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},numbers=left,firstnumber=1272]
\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ **Kuratowski\PYZsq{}s theorem**, minor version. Planarity is a topological notion (an embedding of}
\PY{l+s+sd}{`G` in the plane) which is not part of this file. So we take any property `Planar` of graphs on}
\PY{l+s+sd}{`V` and the theorem of last semester, (1) ↔ (2), as a hypothesis; the conclusion is the minor}
\PY{l+s+sd}{version (1) ↔ (3). This is exactly the logical content of Section 1 of the notes. }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}
\PY{k+kn}{theorem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{kuratowski}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{[}\PY{n}{Fintype}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{V}\PY{o}{]}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{Planar}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{SimpleGraph}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{V}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{→}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k+kt}{Prop}\PY{o}{)}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{kuratowski\PYZus{}subdivision}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∀}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{H}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{SimpleGraph}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{V}\PY{o}{,}
\PY{+w}{      }\PY{n}{Planar}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{H}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{↔}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{¬}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{K5}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{HasSubdivision}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{H}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∧}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{¬}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{K33}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{HasSubdivision}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{H}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n}{Planar}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{↔}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{¬}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{K5}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{IsMinor}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∧}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{¬}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{K33}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{IsMinor}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{by}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nf}{rw}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{[}\PY{n}{kuratowski\PYZus{}subdivision}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{o}{]}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nf}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{no\PYZus{}K5\PYZus{}K33\PYZus{}subdivision\PYZus{}iff\PYZus{}no\PYZus{}K5\PYZus{}K33\PYZus{}minor}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\says Assume Kuratowski's theorem in the subdivision version, (1) $\Leftrightarrow$ (2), for a notion
\lean{Planar} of planarity. Then the minor version (1) $\Leftrightarrow$ (3) holds.

\how Planarity (an embedding in the plane) is topological and not formalised in this file. So
\lean{Planar} is an arbitrary property of graphs, and last semester's theorem is the hypothesis
\lean{kuratowski\_\allowbreak{}subdivision}. \lean{rw [kuratowski\_\allowbreak{}subdivision G]} replaces \lean{Planar G} in the
goal by \lean{¬ K5.HasSubdivision G ∧ ¬ K33.HasSubdivision G}, and the goal is now exactly the
previous theorem.

\tacs \lean{rw} with a hypothesis that is an \lean{↔} (specialised to \lean{G}); \lean{exact}.

\decl{\lean{MinorClosed}, \lean{IsExcludedMinor} (Remark 1.7)}{definitions}{MinorClosed, IsExcludedMinor (Remark 1.7)}
\begin{leancode}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},numbers=left,firstnumber=1289]
\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ `P` is *minor}\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{closed* if every minor of a graph with property `P` has property `P`. }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}
\PY{k+kn}{def}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{MinorClosed}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{P}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∀}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{α}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k+kt}{Type}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{u}\PY{o}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{SimpleGraph}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{α}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{→}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k+kt}{Prop}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k+kt}{Prop}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∀}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{α}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{β}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k+kt}{Type}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{u}\PY{o}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{SimpleGraph}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{α}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{H}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{SimpleGraph}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{β}\PY{o}{)}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{P}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{→}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{H}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{IsMinor}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{→}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{P}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{H}

\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ `H` is an *excluded minor* for `P` if `H` does not have property `P` but every proper minor}
\PY{l+s+sd}{of `H` (a minor not isomorphic to `H`) does. }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}
\PY{k+kn}{def}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{IsExcludedMinor}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{P}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∀}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{α}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k+kt}{Type}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{u}\PY{o}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{SimpleGraph}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{α}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{→}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k+kt}{Prop}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{β}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k+kt}{Type}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{u}\PY{o}{\PYZcb{}}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{H}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{SimpleGraph}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{β}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k+kt}{Prop}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nb+bp}{¬}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{P}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{H}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∧}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∀}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{γ}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k+kt}{Type}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{u}\PY{o}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{H\PYZsq{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{SimpleGraph}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{γ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{H\PYZsq{}}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{IsMinor}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{H}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{→}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{IsEmpty}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{H\PYZsq{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{≃}\PY{n}{g}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{H}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{→}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{P}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{H\PYZsq{}}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\says A class of graphs is a property \lean{P} of graphs on any vertex type of the universe
\lean{Type u}: its type \lean{∀ \{α : Type u\}, SimpleGraph α → Prop} says that \lean{P G} makes sense for
every graph \lean{G}, whatever its vertex type.
\begin{itemize}
\item \lean{MinorClosed P}: every minor \lean{H} of a graph \lean{G} with property \lean{P} has property
  \lean{P}.
\item \lean{IsExcludedMinor P H}: \lean{H} does not have \lean{P}, but every \emph{proper} minor
  \lean{H'} of \lean{H} does. “Proper” means that \lean{H'} is not isomorphic to \lean{H}:
  \lean{IsEmpty (H' ≃g H)}, i.e.\ there is no isomorphism.
\end{itemize}

\decl{\lean{MinorClosed.\allowbreak{}not\_\allowbreak{}isMinor\_\allowbreak{}of\_\allowbreak{}isExcludedMinor}}{theorem}{MinorClosed.not\_isMinor\_of\_isExcludedMinor}
\begin{leancode}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},numbers=left,firstnumber=1299]
\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ Remark 1.7, the easy direction: a graph with property `P` has no excluded minor of `P` as}
\PY{l+s+sd}{a minor. (The converse needs a minimal}\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{counterexample argument over finite graphs.)}

\PY{l+s+sd}{Kuratowski\PYZsq{}s theorem says, in this language, that the excluded minors for planarity are}
\PY{l+s+sd}{exactly `K₅` and `K₃,₃` (Theorem 4.11 of the notes), and the Robertson–Seymour theorem says}
\PY{l+s+sd}{that every minor}\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{closed class has only finitely many excluded minors up to isomorphism. }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}
\PY{k+kn}{theorem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{MinorClosed}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{not\PYZus{}isMinor\PYZus{}of\PYZus{}isExcludedMinor}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{P}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∀}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{α}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k+kt}{Type}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{u}\PY{o}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{SimpleGraph}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{α}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{→}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k+kt}{Prop}\PY{o}{\PYZcb{}}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{hP}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{MinorClosed}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{P}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{α}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{β}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k+kt}{Type}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{u}\PY{o}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{SimpleGraph}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{α}\PY{o}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{H}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{SimpleGraph}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{β}\PY{o}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{hG}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{P}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{o}{)}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{hH}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{IsExcludedMinor}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{P}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{H}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{¬}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{H}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{IsMinor}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{k}{fun}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{↦}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hH}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{l+m}{1}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{hP}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{G}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{H}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hG}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{o}{)}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leancode}
\says If \lean{P} is minor-closed and \lean{G} has \lean{P}, then no excluded minor of \lean{P} is a
minor of \lean{G}.

\how Term mode: \lean{fun h ↦ ...} assumes \lean{h : H.IsMinor G} (to prove a \lean{¬}). Then
\lean{hP G H hG h : P H} by minor-closedness, contradicting \lean{hH.1 : ¬ P H} (the first half of the
\lean{∧} in \lean{IsExcludedMinor}). In this language, Kuratowski's theorem says that the excluded
minors of planarity are exactly $K_5$ and $K_{3,3}$, and the Robertson--Seymour theorem says that
every minor-closed class has only finitely many excluded minors up to isomorphism.

% =====================================================================
\section*{Appendix: glossary of tactics and tools used in Parts 2--8}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Appendix: glossary of tactics and tools}

\subsection*{Tactics}
\begin{description}[style=nextline, leftmargin=1.4em, itemsep=3pt, font=\normalfont]
\item[\tacfont{·} (bullet)] Focus on the first open goal; the indented block must close it. Used after
  every \lean{constructor}, \lean{rcases ... |}, \lean{by\_\allowbreak{}cases}, \lean{refine} with several \lean{?\_\allowbreak{}}.
\item[\tacfont{apply h}] If the goal is the conclusion of \lean{h : A → B → C}, replace it by the
  premises \lean{A}, \lean{B}. With \lean{h : ¬ P} (that is \lean{P → False}) and goal \lean{False}, the new
  goal is \lean{P}. Parts 2, 3, 5, 6.
\item[\tacfont{by\_\allowbreak{}cases h : P}] Two goals: one with \lean{h : P}, one with \lean{h : ¬ P}. Parts 4, 5, 6.
\item[\tacfont{by\_\allowbreak{}contra h}] Prove the goal \lean{P} by assuming \lean{h : ¬ P} and proving \lean{False}.
  Parts 4, 5, 6.
\item[\tacfont{change T} / \tacfont{show T}] Replace the goal by a definitionally equal statement
  \lean{T}, usually to make it readable or to make \lean{rw} see a pattern. Parts 4, 5, 6.
\item[\tacfont{classical}] Treat every proposition as decidable (needed for \lean{if h : P then ...},
  \lean{takeUntil}, finite cardinalities of subtypes). Parts 5, 6.
\item[\tacfont{constructor}] Split a goal \lean{A ∧ B} or \lean{A ↔ B} into two goals. Parts 3, 4, 5, 8.
\item[\tacfont{exact e}] Close the goal with the term \lean{e} (up to definitional unfolding). Everywhere.
\item[\tacfont{exfalso}] Replace the goal by \lean{False}. Part 6.
\item[\tacfont{ext}] Extensionality: two graphs are equal if they have the same edges (Part 3); two
  sets are equal if they have the same elements (Part 4). Patterns like \lean{ext ⟨a, ha⟩} destructure
  the new variables.
\item[\tacfont{grind}] Automation: case splits, propositional logic and reasoning with equalities
  (congruence closure). Used once, in \lean{contractContractIso} (Part 3).
\item[\tacfont{have h : T := e} / \tacfont{have := e}] Prove an intermediate fact and add it to the
  context (without a name it is called \lean{this}). Everywhere.
\item[\tacfont{induction x with | c₁ ... => ... | c₂ ... => ...}] Induction on a walk (Parts 4, 5), on an
  inductive predicate (Part 5), or \lean{using Nat.strong\_\allowbreak{}induction\_\allowbreak{}on} for strong induction on
  \lean{ℕ} (Part 5). \lean{@cons x m y h p ih} names also the implicit arguments of the case.
  Hypotheses that depend on the variable are generalised automatically.
\item[\tacfont{intro x y h}] Introduce the variables/hypotheses of a goal \lean{∀ x, ...} or \lean{A → B}
  (also of \lean{¬ A}, \lean{H ≤ G}, \lean{s ⊆ t}, which unfold to these). Everywhere.
\item[\tacfont{let x : T := e}] A local \emph{definition}: unlike \lean{have}, the value of \lean{x} stays
  visible and Lean may unfold it. Parts 4, 5, 6.
\item[\tacfont{obtain pat := h}] Destructure \lean{h} with the pattern \lean{pat} (like \lean{rcases}). Also
  \lean{obtain ⟨M⟩ := h} for \lean{h : Nonempty \_\allowbreak{}}, and \lean{obtain pat : T := by proof}. Parts 4, 5, 6.
\item[\tacfont{omega}] Proves goals (or finds contradictions) in linear arithmetic over \lean{ℕ} and
  \lean{ℤ}, treating other terms (like \lean{Fintype.card β}) as unknowns. Parts 4, 5.
\item[\tacfont{push Not at h}] Move \lean{¬} inwards: \lean{¬ ∃ x, P x} becomes \lean{∀ x, ¬ P x},
  \lean{¬ ∀ x, P x} becomes \lean{∃ x, ¬ P x}, \lean{¬ a ≠ b} becomes \lean{a = b}. Part 5.
\item[\tacfont{rcases h with pat}] Take \lean{h} apart. Patterns: \lean{⟨p, q⟩} for \lean{∧}, \lean{∃},
  structures and subtypes; \lean{p | q} for \lean{∨} (and \lean{p | q | r} for \lean{A ∨ B ∨ C});
  \lean{-} to clear a component; \lean{\_\allowbreak{}} to keep it unnamed. Parts 2, 3, 5, 6, 8.
\item[\tacfont{refine e}] Like \lean{exact}, but \lean{e} may contain holes \lean{?\_\allowbreak{}}, which become new
  goals. Used to build \lean{⟨⟨B, ?\_\allowbreak{}, ?\_\allowbreak{}, ?\_\allowbreak{}, ?\_\allowbreak{}⟩⟩} models. Parts 3--6.
\item[\tacfont{right} (\tacfont{left})] Prove \lean{A ∨ B} by proving \lean{B} (resp.\ \lean{A}). Part 6.
\item[\tacfont{rintro pat}] \lean{intro} followed by \lean{rcases}, with the same patterns. Parts 3--8.
\item[\tacfont{rw [h₁, ← h₂] (at h)}] Rewrite with equations or \lean{↔}s, left to right (or right to
  left with \lean{←}), in the goal (or in \lean{h}); afterwards \lean{rw} tries \lean{rfl}, which also uses
  \lean{@[refl]} lemmas such as \lean{Reachable.refl}. Everywhere.
\item[\tacfont{simp only [l₁, l₂, ...]}] Rewrite repeatedly with exactly these lemmas (plus basic
  simplifications such as \lean{⟨a, h⟩.1 = a}). Part 3.
\item[\tacfont{sorry}] Close a goal without proof (a warning is printed). Part 7.
\item[\tacfont{specialize h a b}] Replace \lean{h : ∀ x y, P x y} by \lean{h : P a b}. Part 2.
\item[\tacfont{subst h}] For \lean{h : x = e} (or \lean{e = x}) with a local variable \lean{x}: replace
  \lean{x} by \lean{e} everywhere and remove it. Parts 4, 6.
\item[\tacfont{suffices h : P from e}] Reduce the goal to \lean{P}; \lean{e} proves the old goal from
  \lean{h}. Part 5.
\end{description}

\subsection*{Term-mode tools}
\begin{description}[style=nextline, leftmargin=1.4em, itemsep=3pt, font=\normalfont]
\item[\lean{⟨a, b, ...⟩}] Anonymous constructor: proves \lean{∧}, \lean{∃}, \lean{↔}; builds subtype
  elements, structures (\lean{Model}, \lean{Copy}, homomorphisms) and \lean{Nonempty}. Nested ones may be
  flattened.
\item[\lean{fun x ↦ e}] A function; also a proof of \lean{∀ x, ...}, of \lean{A → B} and of \lean{¬ A}.
\item[\lean{Iff.rfl}, \lean{rfl}] Proof of \lean{P ↔ P}, \lean{a = a}, accepted up to unfolding definitions
  and proof irrelevance.
\item[\lean{Or.inl}, \lean{Or.inr}] Prove \lean{A ∨ B} from \lean{A}, resp.\ \lean{B}.
\item[\lean{h.1}, \lean{h.2}] The two directions of an \lean{↔}, the two halves of an \lean{∧}, the value
  and the proof of a subtype element.
\item[\lean{h.symm}, \lean{h.trans h'}] Symmetry and transitivity (of \lean{=}, \lean{≠} (symm only),
  \lean{Reachable}, \lean{IsMinor}, \lean{IsMinorOp}).
\item[\lean{absurd h hn}] From \lean{h : P} and \lean{hn : ¬ P}, anything.
\item[\lean{h ▸ e}] Rewrite the type of \lean{e} with the equation \lean{h} (in whichever direction fits).
\item[\lean{Subtype.ext}, \lean{congrArg f h}] \lean{a.1 = b.1 → a = b}; \lean{x = y → f x = f y}.
\item[\lean{h.elim f}] For \lean{h : Nonempty α}: use an element \lean{f : α → p} to prove a proposition.
\item[\lean{.some}, \lean{.choose}] Choose an element of a nonempty set / a witness of an \lean{∃}
  (axiom of choice); \lean{.some\_\allowbreak{}mem}, \lean{.choose\_\allowbreak{}spec} are their properties.
\item[\lean{\{ e with field := ... \}}] Structure update: copy the fields of \lean{e}, add or change some.
\item[\lean{f (x := v)}] Named argument: give the argument called \lean{x} explicitly.
\item[\lean{\#check @c}] Print the type of \lean{c} with all implicit arguments.
\item[\lean{variable (G) in}] Make \lean{G} explicit for the next declaration only.
\end{description}

\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{@{}lL@{}}
\toprule
lemma & statement\\
\midrule
\lean{deleteEdges\_\allowbreak{}adj} & \lean{(G.deleteEdges s).Adj a b ↔ G.Adj a b ∧ s(a, b) ∉ s}\\
\lean{Set.disjoint\_\allowbreak{}left} & \lean{Disjoint s t ↔ ∀ a ∈ s, a ∉ t}\\
\lean{Set.mem\_\allowbreak{}singleton\_\allowbreak{}iff} & \lean{a ∈ \{b\} ↔ a = b}\\
\lean{connected\_\allowbreak{}iff\_\allowbreak{}exists\_\allowbreak{}forall\_\allowbreak{}reachable} & \lean{G.Connected ↔ ∃ v, ∀ w, G.Reachable v w}\\
\lean{induce\_\allowbreak{}pair\_\allowbreak{}connected\_\allowbreak{}of\_\allowbreak{}adj} & \lean{G.Adj u v → (G.induce \{u, v\}).Connected}\\
\lean{Connected.map} & a surjective homomorphic image of a connected graph is connected\\
\lean{Reachable.map} & a homomorphism maps reachable pairs to reachable pairs\\
\lean{Walk.exists\_\allowbreak{}boundary\_\allowbreak{}dart} & a walk from inside \lean{S} to outside \lean{S} has a dart leaving \lean{S}\\
\lean{Walk.take\_\allowbreak{}spec} & \lean{(p.takeUntil u h).append (p.dropUntil u h) = p}\\
\lean{Walk.support\_\allowbreak{}append} & \lean{(p.append q).support = p.support ++ q.support.tail}\\
\lean{List.disjoint\_\allowbreak{}of\_\allowbreak{}nodup\_\allowbreak{}append} & \lean{(l₁ ++ l₂).Nodup → Disjoint l₁ l₂}\\
\lean{Sym2.eq\_\allowbreak{}iff} & \lean{s(a, b) = s(c, d) ↔ (a = c ∧ b = d) ∨ (a = d ∧ b = c)}\\
\lean{Sym2.mem\_\allowbreak{}iff} & \lean{a ∈ s(b, c) ↔ a = b ∨ a = c}\\
\lean{Fintype.card\_\allowbreak{}le\_\allowbreak{}of\_\allowbreak{}injective} & an injection \lean{α → β} gives \lean{card α ≤ card β}\\
\lean{Fintype.card\_\allowbreak{}subtype\_\allowbreak{}lt} & \lean{¬ p x → card \{x // p x\} < card α}\\
\lean{Copy.ofLE}, \lean{Copy.induce}, \lean{Iso.toCopy} & copies from \lean{≤}, from \lean{induce}, from an isomorphism\\
\bottomrule
